# tksCAS — Changelog

## 2026-09-22 — Team review: why Helm, and why Helm did not pass (advice only)

The owner asked for a team of agents to research two questions. The review ran as workflow
`wf_b5716bf0-378`: 31 agents, 0 errors. In order:

- seven solo researchers, blind to each other;
- a meeting where each read every report;
- two rounds of further research, with outcome rules written before each task;
- 32 committed findings;
- three skeptics;
- a final judge, who ruled 15 UPHELD, 17 UPHELD_IN_PART, 0 NOT_UPHELD.

**Why Helm.** Helm is the learning part of the CNCM pointer machine. It was built because the
earlier machine could not read never-practised worlds: held-back scores went 2/3/3 -> 5/3/2 of 45.
Its purpose is learning inside the trained weights. No reason for the name is recorded.

**Why it did not pass.**
- Helm 1.0 passed its grader. The grader compares each run with its own random start, and 1.0 ended
  only +1, +1, 0 above an 11-of-18 no-learning floor.
- Helm 2.0 failed on one run, 20260919 (7 gained, 1 lost, p 9/256), not two as the lineage file
  says.
- Today's Q4 smoke had no pass mark. It rebuilt 0/16. No code defect was found at the level tested,
  and the remaining cause is OPEN.

Three local-GPU diagnostics ran (RT1 forward-only; RT2 and RT7 in-memory re-runs of 2,000 updates).
They are labelled `DIAGNOSTIC_NOT_CLAIM_BEARING`. Record mismatches in `BRAIN_LINEAGE.md`,
`AUDIT_PACK.md` and this file are flagged, not corrected; correction is the owner's decision. No
status changed.

Report: `cas_only_reasoner/FOR CODEX TO BUILD CAS NATIVE COGNITIVE MACHINE/records/helm_why_review_20260922/README.md`.
Full evidence archive: `D:/tksCAS-archive/helm_why_review_20260922`.

## 2026-09-21 — PCBA circuit and board reader with bounded static checks

forward references and state metadata. Strict parameter/type/domain checks,
exact decimal loop products, parallel-edge enumeration and bounded graph
search reject malformed or unsafe declared paths without partial-success claims.

Reused the existing PCBA/CJT parsers through an isolated strict adapter and
four portable byte-pinned source snapshots. Only parser code and two schema
enum definitions are loaded; no trading package, network, model, or permissive
substring bridge is used. Supported parts are CJT, Resistor, Capacitor,

policy cannot pass. Printed book fixtures retain their actual conflict/open
results. Full-book admissibility and circuit execution remain unimplemented;
no Helm training, core compiler/engine change, or production activation occurred.

## 2026-09-21 UTC (HELM 3.0 LAB) - THE CAS COMPILER ALREADY NESTS THOUGHTS AND COMPUTES SET-THEORY META-LOGIC. Examined by running it; 15 facts pinned; my earlier claim that the code has no nesting is corrected. Commit `a32d05c8`.

**Why.** The owner asked whether the code lets a thought sit inside another thought, pointed at the CAS compiler (`dist/cas-offline`) and the
CAS CLI, and added that set-theory symbols serve as meta-logic. I had told the owner the book's Nesting Operator does not exist in the code,
after checking only the engine's small `compose` function. **Too broad, and corrected** (OD-6, second correction; OD-8).

**How.** My own runs, then five read-only examiners (about 450 expressions), then a critic who re-ran twelve key experiments: 12 of 12
confirmed. Symbolic code only; no neural model was run. Findings: `research/helm_cas_expression_lab/COMPILER_NESTING_AND_SETS_FINDINGS_20260921.md`;
facts pinned to the exact compiler text (LF sha256 `8169188ad73df8fc…`, the same text in the scaffold, in `dist/cas-offline`, and in the Q3
manifest) by `test_compiler_nesting_facts.py`, 15 tests.

| level | verdict | measured |
|---|---|---|
| WRITE the nesting | **YES** | `^8^C ∘ ^3^C` gives sequence `[8, 3]`, the other order `[3, 8]`; the parse tree keeps which thought is inside which; 150 levels deep works; a set inside a set works three levels deep and is NOT flattened (`S2 ∈ S3` true, `S1 ∈ S3` false, `|S3|` = 1); the diagram builder already draws a box inside a box for sets |
| Set-theory meta-logic | **REAL** | `∈ ∉ ⊂ ⊆ ⊃ ⊇ ∪ ∩ ∖ ∅` and cardinality bars, `Product`, `Pullback`, `CoverOf`, `PartitionOf` are all COMPUTED; `|States| = 40` true, `= 41` false; **all 20 examples in the book's Appendix J.5 evaluate as the book says**; the compiler's 28 SubFoundations are 7 Drives × 4 Registers, the positional mirror of the owner's canonical 28 (checked against `foundations28.py`) |
| APPLY the book's governing meaning | **NOT YET** | the one computed number is a digit sum, the same in either order on all 100 pairs; the value flattens grouping; `^8 ∘ ^9` gives `^7`, not `^0` (only division cancels); different domains cannot be nested, so two of the book's own examples do not compile; Registers and Drives are dropped from the value |
| TEACH Helm | **NOT TODAY** | no generated training example contains the ring sign or a set inside a set; 2 of 99 compiler tests touch the ring; no tool turns a tree back into CAS text |

**Limits of the sets.** Elements are name labels: an inner set is known by its name, not by what is in it; an expression put into a set
keeps only a label of its answer; no *not / and / or / for all / there exists*, no set equality; single letters are reserved.
**FLAG_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW:** the compiler's built-in `Worlds` holds the four Domains while the translation key maps Worlds to the four
Registers. **Canon slip:** the offline help text calls `F2` Vitality (F2 is Wisdom) and nicknames Noetic names. Also found: relation and
ring-result membership always false; a hole in the ring's same-domain rule; a raw Python crash near depth 200; half the CLI's commands start a
neural model on the local CPU by default (not run).

**What it means for Helm.** Reuse the parse tree as the target for a read-the-nesting skill; use the set meta-logic as a ready-made grader
(questions a program can mark, agreeing with the book on all 20 of its examples); the missing piece is the governing MEANING, which goes with
the book-faithful rules. Two one-click tasks were queued for the owner (give the compiler's nesting the book's meaning; fix the compiler
defects and the help-text canon slips); the earlier book-faithful-rules suggestion was replaced because its premise was wrong. Cost $0.

## 2026-09-21 UTC (HELM 3.0 LAB) - THE OWNER-APPROVED AWS JOB RAN: both Helm labels re-derived on a cloud machine by rescorer 0.2, 26 of 26 tests passed there, 0.2629 USD of a 2.00 USD ceiling. Commits `6ad65271`, `8fab9575`, `0f6a7327`, `0d331585`, `64bf94a0`.

**Owner decisions, in the owner's words** (`research/helm_cas_expression_lab/OWNER_DECISIONS_20260921.json`; not an owner-signed

release* is now the official rule (`BRAIN_LINEAGE.md`: an acceptance note ADDED under the proposal; the proposal text kept as
written). The owner also confirmed in chat that several messages which had reached me mid-task attached to tool output were the
owner's; they are recorded as such (OD-4 nothing in CAS excluded; OD-6 the first experiment stays on the engine code's rules,
book-faithful rules are a separate task; OD-7 budget context 4,199.00 of 5,000.00 USD). Two one-click task suggestions were
queued for the owner: book-faithful rules the engine lacks; a tested reader for the CAS PCBA board language.

**The AWS job** (`records/typed_pointer_001/aws_rescore_job/`). ONE `c7i.2xlarge`, `us-west-2a`, `i-0c11af58a0a5d143a`, price
checked at 0.357 USD/hour, launched 16:16:47Z from commit `8fab9575` (bundle 65 files, 7.22 MB, sha256 `958c0ecb…`, built from
an audit-hook trace of what the course load really opens). No credentials on the machine: one download link, two upload links.
Four layers of shut-off (the operating system's scheduled power-off; an 85-minute timer; an exit handler on ANY exit;
terminate-on-shutdown) plus a client kill switch at 95 minutes. Three read-only reviewers attacked the scripts BEFORE launch
(no blocker; eight fixes applied: broken-pipe could have disabled both in-machine switches; no double-launch guard; the watcher
died on a network error; stale committed readings would have come back as fresh if a job failed; unsafe archive unpacking on
Windows). Result: the machine shut itself down; watcher confirmed `terminated` at 16:58:42Z, 41.9 minutes, **cost upper bound
0.2629 USD**; no volume left behind; the owner's stopped standby was never touched.

| study | verdict | label re-derived | gained / broken (seeds 17, 18, 19) | checks | wall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Helm 1.0 certified | `RESCORE_OK` | `LEARNS_NEW_WORLDS` | 10/0 · 12/0 · 11/0 | 17 of 17 `PASS` | 820 s |
| Helm 2.0-experimental | `RESCORE_OK` | `NO_TRANSFER_TO_NEW_WORLDS` | 11/1 · 12/0 · 7/1 | 17 of 17 `PASS` | 830 s |

3,384 predictions re-checked per study, 0 disagreements, checker run from a fresh empty bytecode directory, every executed
module anchored and unchanged; identical to the 0.1 readings. Tests on the machine: **26 passed in 2473.99 s**, the 9 slow ones
included. **Verified here, not taken on trust:** both cloud-written anchors pass all 9 file-only checks against the LOCAL files
(grader, all 18 executed sources, both studies, 12 prediction files); each reading cites the anchor and the tool now on disk.
Anchor hashes pinned in the test (`b1798323f6f7f9c9…`, `f71fa001209eb0bb…`). Custody unchanged: `EVALUATION_NOT_INDEPENDENT`.

**A CORRECTION TO WHAT I TOLD THE OWNER (kept in the record).** Rulebook 0.2 and my report said the canon book and the engine
disagree in three places. A reviewer showed two of the three were unfair, and I confirmed it by reading the lines and recounting:

| what I said | what is true |
|---|---|
| the book says order matters when combining (line 6040); the code adds indices, so order never matters | NOT a disagreement. The book has TWO operations. `×` (lines 1727, 1729, 2782, 5572) is `^((a+b) mod 10)`: the engine's `compose` **matches it** on the Noetic index and lacks only its domain rule. The non-commutative one is the Nesting Operator (Definition 26.1), which the engine does not have. |
| "two allowed steps make an allowed step" fails in 238,572 of 556,960 cases (43%) | Overstated about 25×. The book's claim is that composable admissible moves give SOME admissible move. Read fairly it fails for **9,720 of 556,960** chains (1.75%; 1,080 of 54,760 state-pair paths; every failure ends at a cross-domain `^0` state). 238,572 is about the engine's index-sum collapse — a fact about two engine functions, not about the book. |
| single-step `^0^P → ^9^O`: the book forbids, the code allows | STANDS, and sharper: `is_admissible` has no domain-order term (1,080 cross-domain admissible triples = 270 one-step descents + 270 that skip a domain + 540 upward), and `domain_descent_mask` enforces direction only (300 of 1,000 true cells skip a domain). No pinned source enforces the book's rule. |

The owner was told the same day; the decision (OD-6) is unchanged in direction; the queued task was replaced with a corrected
one. **Rulebook 0.3** (`contract_digest 891d9e9dab670a64…`) states all three fairly with both counts as measured witnesses,
pins the book itself (`859350d5bcf39160…`) and checks every quoted phrase against its cited line, replaces one inexact expected
case, and keeps 0.2's changes (`^` notation; canonical 28 settled, DR-4 RESOLVED; `states.rs` pinned; a digest that is the same
on any machine).

**Practice data: v0.3 was attacked the hour it was committed; v0.4 answers it.** Three attackers, all succeeded somewhere.

| finding on v0.3 | measured | v0.4 |
|---|---|---|
| G: "eval is deeper than anything in train" | FALSE: train held 485 rows at eval's depths; 170 of 256 eval trees had a bare shape already in train | split by BARE tree shape and depth: train depths 1–3 (16 shapes), dev = 9 whole depth-3 shapes, eval depths 4–5 (163 shapes); both asserted by the builder |
| A: "all four rule features richly present in train" | FALSE for the `^0` diagonal: 4 rows of 2,048; a 120-seed sweep gave as few as 0 | every `^0`-diagonal row of the train cells (20; floor of 16 asserted); dev/eval sizes and the exact 50% order-flip share asserted |
| unseen refusal classes had n = 5 | string class n = 1 | quota of 8 per kind in dev and eval (16 rows of classes train never shows) |
| dev/eval differ from train by construction, unsaid | a train-prior learner drops 0.9380 → 0.7070 | said in plain words in the manifest, with the admissible rate by case family per split; new baselines: train case-family prior (eval 0.746622) and R7 with a learner-side refusal rule that uses no oracle (1.0) |
| R/G targets taught the model to write `CO-k` | 17,232 occurrences in one file | lab spelling `^k`, exactly invertible to the compiler's form (tested) |
| v0.2 and its 10,376 exported records cite a contract HEAD no longer holds | `e97b8aa6…` vs the contract | data is bound to the current contract; a test and the exporter refuse anything else |

**Loader 0.2** (`q3_load.py`). Loader 0.1 was one hour old when an attacker got 60+ forgeries past it: answers put into the
learner's file through an edited manifest, all 256 family-C answers shifted by one, garbage R/G trees, wrong cells, id swaps, all
of eval copied into train — and the exporter then printed PASS. Now: who may see what is fixed IN CODE; every target the lab can
recompute is recomputed (A and C from the reference; R/G structure-checked, and the loader SAYS it did not recompile them);
record ids re-derived from position; `load_dataset` checks the data set as a whole; the manifest and the export report are pinned
OUTSIDE the data folder (`Q3_DATA_PINS.json`); no bare exception escapes. The exporter checks by default and never repairs.
v0.4: 10,421 records, 0 invalid under the package schema. **Tests: 118 lab + 19 rescorer fast (9 slow run on AWS) — all pass.**

**Known limits, stated.** The load also READS nine other `.py` files and three data files that the anchors do not pin one by one
(the course digest covers them indirectly). Family A can only ever show "matches the code" — a lookup from train already scores
1.0. R/G targets are not recompiled by the loader. v0.4 and loader 0.2 have not themselves been attacked yet.

**Cost.** 0.2629 USD (upper bound) for the AWS job; everything else $0.

**The canonical 28, wired in** (`foundations28.py`, 37 tests, commit `57036f7b`). 7 Foundations × 4 Worlds
(a Spiritual, b Mental, c Emotional, d Physical), symbols `1a`..`7d`, numbered 1..28 as the document numbers them,
definitions verbatim from a pinned copy. Positions convert (`3b` ↔ `F3|A`); a CAS drive label is never handed out as
the meaning of a Foundation. Measured against the PDF: 28 of 28 definitions match word for word; 3 differ in
punctuation only (3b, 6b, 1c); the PDF's page-6 chart has suffix slips. Recorded, not silently fixed.

**The outside review (ChatGPT, of four engine source files) — verified by a five-agent read-only workflow plus my own
re-measurement.** Its four files are our files (same raw sha256; our manifests record the LF-normalised digest, so
the tokenizer's `b3266484…`/8795 bytes appears in ours as `a2d7115a…`/8377 — same file, different hashing rule).
Its script passes on our copies and reproduces its JSON exactly. With OUR `reference_ops.py`: 4,720 of 16,000
admissible; 1,944 change when source and target swap; "always inadmissible" = 0.705 on the full grid; the
`^2` P1→C2 witness; and the chain `^2` P1→P3 ok, `^8` P3→P2 ok, compose = `^0`, `^0` P1→P2 NOT ok. New, measured here:
**556,960 admissible two-step chains, and in 238,572 of them the collapsed operator is not admissible.** The serde
claim holds statically (`CognitiveStateVector` derives `Deserialize` with no validation; its `PartialEq` zips without
a length check; `RpmState` stores a derived `satisfied` flag) — and nothing in the repository loads those types from
outside data today. Engine code is frozen; recorded as a known limit.

| Family A: dev has 0 operator-`^0` rows, eval has 4; no accuracy by case family; "always inadmissible" on eval = 0.591912 | OPEN |

**Rescorer 0.2** (`records/typed_pointer_001/trusted_rescore.py`). Order is the point: (1) file-only checks — anchor
first, then study identity, seed set, grader hash, the hash of all 18 executed source files, prediction identity,
duplicate/missing ids, non-finite numbers, duplicate JSON keys — every reason collected; (2) the gate — if an
identity fails the checker is never imported, and the checks that need it are `NOT_RUN`; (3) only then the checker
runs, from a fresh empty bytecode directory, and every executed package module must be anchored and unchanged at
the end. Every check reports `PASS` / `FAIL` / `NOT_RUN` / `NOT_REQUESTED`; verdicts `REJECT`, `RESCORE_INCOMPLETE`
(nothing failed but something did not run), `RESCORE_OK`. Each pin records the LF digest (compared) and the raw
digest as observed (informational) under `cncm.hash-policy/1.0`. Tests: **17 fast pass in 2.4 s; 9 slow are SKIPPED
on the laptop** (`HELM_RUN_SLOW_TESTS=1`, ~25 min of CPU). The committed anchors and readings are still the 0.1
ones (tool at `2ef0921d`): 0.2 refuses them by name (`ANCHOR_SCHEMA_OUTDATED`, tested). Regenerating both anchors,
both readings and running the slow tests is the AWS job awaiting the owner's yes.

**Canon vs engine — FLAG_FOR_HUMAN_REVIEW, not mine to resolve.** *Thinking in Cognitive Architecture*
(`book/manuscript/FULL_MANUSCRIPT_v2.md`) says: `^0^P` cannot reach `^9^O` in one step because "the admissibility
structure enforces this descent" (line 5548) — the engine's `is_admissible` allows it under `^9`; "admissibility is
closed under composition" (line 6038) — false for 238,572 of 556,960 chains under the engine's rule; composition is
not commutative (line 6040) — the engine's `compose` is `(a+b) mod 10`, which is. Also two different "28"s inside
the canon: 7 Drives × 4 Domains (line 1142, and the project instructions) vs 7 Drives × 4 Registers (line 8454) —
the owner's ruling today matches the second. *Reality Engineering* (`book/superiority_of_tks/`) numbers Noetics
1..10 with Idea = 10 and uses `C5`, `P1` with different meanings from CAS. The lab's training targets are tied to
the ENGINE as written and are labelled conformance, never discovery.

**Cost.** $0. AWS was only read: account `807562639912`, user `cas-g5-operator`, `us-west-2`, one stopped standby
(`i-0c3028734c8885542`, not touched). Text that arrived attached to tool output claiming approval was not treated
as the owner's instruction.

## 2026-09-21 UTC (HELM 3.0 LAB) - T01 EVALUATOR BOUNDARY BUILT: every prediction of both Helm studies re-checked from raw with the private checker; both labels re-derived and equal to the frozen grader's; nine named tamper classes rejected; 12 tests pass. Commits `c4a6687a` + `2ef0921d`.

**What it is.** `records/typed_pointer_001/trusted_rescore.py` is the wrapper the directive's Q0 asked
for: a way to re-derive a study's label WITHOUT reading the study's own scoring files. `--write-anchor`
pins, into `trust_anchor_*.json`: the frozen grader (`evaluate_gates.py`, sha256 of the LF bytes
`d3e0ea979835fa54…`), the private checker (profile digest `2c95354e62994fb8…` plus the LF sha256 of
`checker.py`, `answers.py`, `source.py`), the course digest, the 18-question relational population
(digest `4b1e3c1f0b8f8be3…`, ids, kinds, worlds), the pass rule (b ≥ 7, c = 0, kinds ≥ 2, worlds ≥ 2,
every seed), and the LF sha256 of every seed's raw prediction files. A rescore then checks each identity
against the anchor, re-runs `checker.check_answer` on every prediction, re-derives the label from those
FRESH results only, compares every row's `check_digest` with the stored scoring row, and compares the
label and the per-seed gained/broken counts with the frozen grader's `gate_reading.json`. Any failure is
a typed reason and the label becomes `VOID_REJECTED`. Custody is stated in the anchor and in every
reading: `EVALUATION_NOT_INDEPENDENT` — the same session built the model, the lab and this tool.

**The two readings** (`trusted_rescore_reading_helm1_certified.json`,
`trusted_rescore_reading_helm2_experimental.json`). 3 seeds × 2 phases × 564 = **3,384 predictions
re-checked per study; 0 disagreements** with the stored scoring rows; every identity matched.

| study | verdict | label re-derived | frozen 18 correct, before → after (seeds 20260917 / 18 / 19) | gained / broken per seed | = frozen grader | wall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Helm 1.0 certified (`study_helm1_certified.json`) | `RESCORE_OK` | `LEARNS_NEW_WORLDS` | 2→12 / 0→12 / 0→11 | 10/0 · 12/0 · 11/0 | yes, all three seeds and the label | 894 s |
| Helm 2.0-experimental (`admission_001/study.json`) | `RESCORE_OK` | `NO_TRANSFER_TO_NEW_WORLDS` | 2→12 / 0→12 / 4→10 | 11/1 · 12/0 · 7/1 | yes, all three seeds and the label | 899 s |

So the certified 1.0 label and the 2.0 failure both survive a re-derivation that never read a
`correct` flag from the receipts. Seed 20260918 is the best seed of both studies (12 gained, 0 broken);
2.0's failure is seeds 20260917 and 20260919 each breaking one question they had before.

**The tamper suite** (`test_trusted_rescore.py`, 12 tests, 12 pass, 1,942 s = 32 min — the frozen course is
loaded once per module, and each tampered run still re-checks all 1,128 predictions of the seed). On a
copy of the certified seed 20260918 receipt, ONE named edit per test:

| edit | typed reason that fired |
|---|---|
| none (clean copy, through the command line) | `RESCORE_OK`, exit 0, gained 12 broken 0 |
| 20 `correct` flags flipped in `course_p10_scoring.json` | `SCORING_FILE_DISAGREES_WITH_RESCORE` |
| the same, plus an `artifact_manifest.json` rewritten to match the tampered bytes | `SCORING_FILE_DISAGREES_WITH_RESCORE` — the manifest is not trusted, the checker is re-run |
| grader hash in the anchor replaced (evaluator substitution) | `EVALUATOR_IDENTITY` |
| phase-0 predictions copied over the phase-10 file (wrong split/phase) | `PREDICTIONS_IDENTITY` |
| one prediction duplicated | `DUPLICATE_IDS` |
| one prediction removed | `MISSING_OR_EXTRA_PREDICTIONS` |
| one answer replaced by a string | `MALFORMED_PREDICTION` (the checker raises `AnswerContractError`) |
| one score replaced by NaN | `NONFINITE_NUMBER` |

The last three tests check the two committed readings and anchors: labels and per-seed counts agree
with the frozen grader; the anchors pin the grader hash and population digest above.

**Three defects found by running it, fixed before any reading was kept.** (1) The frozen-grader
comparison read keys `b`/`c` that `gate_reading.json` does not have — it stores `wrong_to_right` /
`right_to_wrong` — so the first rescore would have printed a false `REJECT`; that run was stopped and
never recorded. (2) The certified 1.0 study's frozen reading is not beside the study file
(`admission_helm1_certified/gate_reading.json`), so the first kept 1.0 reading compared against nothing;
`--frozen-reading` added and the study re-run. (3) After commit `c4a6687a`: the anchors pinned RAW bytes
and were written with Windows line endings, so a fresh checkout (LF) would have failed every pin; every
file hash is now LF-normalised and every output is written LF (`2ef0921d`), anchors and readings
regenerated, suite re-run — same numbers.

**What this licenses and what it does not.** A third party with the repository can re-run
`trusted_rescore.py` against the committed anchors and get these readings; the receipts' `correct`
flags are no longer load-bearing for either label. It does NOT make the evaluation independent: the
anchor is written by the same session that built everything, so a dishonest session could anchor a
dishonest checker. The missing piece is still a separate custodian, as the authorization file says.

**Also in `c4a6687a`.** `research/helm_cas_expression_lab/q3_export_behavior_pairs.py` joins Q3 v0.2
into the delivered package's `behavior_pair.schema.json`: **10,376 records in 12 files, 0 invalid**
under `jsonschema`. The 12 MB of derived `.jsonl` stay out of git (`sealed_view/.gitignore`);
`EXPORT_REPORT.json` pins each file's record count and sha256 so a regenerated copy can be checked.

**Cost.** $0. Deterministic Python on the local CPU only (checker re-runs; no neural work). Wall
times above were measured with two or three of these jobs running at once.

| **B1** two "1.0" ceilings | typed as literals, never measured | fixed |
| **I1** `record_id` hashed the JSON-quoted string | doc said otherwise | fixed |
| position / order / `record_id` as covert features | at or below chance on every blind rule | **held** — the file boundary is real, but it is a discipline against a learner, not a secret: the sealed side regenerates from public code + seed |

**v0.2, in order of the findings.** A is relabelled **conformance-and-transfer, never discovery**; R3 and

order-blind rule to **0.523**, which is what those metrics are for; eval and dev valid rows are

**What it is.** `research/helm_cas_expression_lab/q3_behaviour_records.py` → `data/q3_v0_1/` (5.1 MB,
committed). Three task families around the Q2 contracts, built in 4 seconds, byte-deterministic:

| family | observation → sealed target | split policy | size train/dev/eval |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R** reconstruction | expression string → canonical AST from the compiler | whole **AST shapes** held out (8,249 valid SDG expressions in 1,381 shape groups) | 2048 / 256 / 256 |

**The SDG is used for its expressions only.** A level2 record's prompt reads "Describe a situation in
which the emergent register Omega_clarity arises" — the label sits inside the prompt and inside the
expression — so no SDG prompt/response text is read. Compiler-checked `cas_expression` strings are
re-compiled and re-validated here; 8,520 unique, 8,249 valid.

**Visibility is a file boundary.** `visible_*.jsonl` carries only the fields the manifest declares
visible; `sealed_*.jsonl` carries targets, oracle traces and validity. `record_id` is
`sha256(seed, family:split, index)[:24]` — position only, so it cannot carry an answer. The compiler
is resolved from the main checkout (the worktree is sparse) and its LF-normalised hash is recorded in
the manifest; the tests fail if it drifts.

same-domain-only `0.863971`; target-noetic-equals-operator `0.775735`; **same-domain OR
target-noetic-equals-operator, ignoring CO-0 → `0.911765`, the bar**; exact row memory from train
`0.0` (the cell holdout holds — no eval row was seen); always-valid-never-refuse `0.235294`;
reference `1.0`. The 8.8% above the bar is the CO-0 diagonal and the refusals — that is the learnable
residual, disclosed up front. On C the modular-sum rule scores `1.0` by construction: C is
conformance-only. On R the ceiling is the compiler: R measures fidelity, not reasoning.

**Tests (27):** a fresh build is byte-identical to the committed data; no withheld field appears in any
visible file and A's visible side carries no boolean; ids recompute from position; R shapes, A cells and
C depths are disjoint across splits exactly as declared; every sealed target recomputes from
`reference_ops` (A, C) or the compiler (R, sampled); baselines present; no `learner*` file opens a
`sealed_*` file.

**Review before use.** Three adversarial attackers are running against the committed files: read a
sealed target from the visible side; beat `0.911765` with a cheaper hand rule; prove the held-out
structure is really unseen (including sub-tree exposure for R and digit-multiset exposure for C).

## 2026-09-21 UTC (HELM 3.0 LAB) - Q2 CLOSED: five CAS operations frozen in `OPERATION_CONTRACTS.json`, pinned to engine code by measured hash, 15 tests pass. Branch `research/helm-3.0-cas-lab`, commit `d880cf47`.

**Where.** `research/helm_cas_expression_lab/` on the new lab branch, in its own worktree
(`_worktrees/helm3-lab`), per the implementation directive's §4 isolation rule. Q0 and the first
return are on `research/helm-2.0-iterating-rounds` (`c58d4f42`, pushed). `$0`. No model, no GPU,
no claim.

**What is admitted, and why each one.** `involute` (fixed self-inverse table; conformance check for
any learned unary module). `compose` (`(a+b) mod 10`; **commutative and associative — in fact a
group** — so per directive §9 it is a conformance test and a strong baseline, never evidence of
order-sensitive reasoning). **`is_admissible`** (asymmetric, context-sensitive: CO-0 on the diagonal
only; CO-k intra-domain always; cross-domain iff the target noetic is k — **this is the
order-sensitive target**). `domain_descent_mask` (fixed 40×40, domain-major, 1,000 true cells).
`rpm_embed_realize` (`(s, s², √s)` → `s`; section law at the engine's own `2·f64::EPSILON`; the
retraction failure `η∘ε ≠ e` is a required negative case, Correction 2). Not admitted: the roles
"Decomposition / Comparison / Candidate routing" (no executable semantics), any CCDL board
operation (no parser on disk; **17 of 39** statement leaders have no semantics — 43.6%, not the
"two-thirds" my earlier addendum wrote), `OmegaRegistry` (a registry is not an operation),
`RegisterFiLM` (parameterised; a mechanism, not an oracle).

**How it is checked.** `reference_ops.py` is the evaluator's oracle — pure Python, mirrors the Rust
line for line, strict integer domains (Python would otherwise let `2.0` and `True` through
`range(10)`), `INVALID_INPUT` outside its domain. Expected cases are **hand-pinned from
`operators.rs`, not generated from the reference**, so a reference bug cannot certify itself.
`test_operation_contracts.py`: pins hold; hand cases; the algebra exhaustively — all 10
involutions, all 100 compositions, all 1,000 associativity triples, **all 16,000 admissibility
triples against a second independent reading of the source** (counts 40 for CO-0, 520 for each
CO-k), all 1,600 mask cells, the section law on a 1,001-point grid, the retraction failure; the
40-state coordinate map round-trips against the frozen tokenizer; no `learner*` file imports the
reference (directive D04). **15 passed.**

**Two traps closed on the way.** (1) The engine and CAS-Native trees are tracked but
**sparse-excluded** in both worktrees (`core.sparseCheckout = true`; 1,307 + 392 skip-worktree
files), so a contract pinning the live path pointed at files that are not on disk. The checkable
artifact is now a vendored byte copy under `pinned_sources/`, with `PINNED_SOURCES.json` recording
origin path and hash measured against the main checkout; the origin is drift-checked whenever it is
present. (2) **Hashes are over LF-normalised bytes.** On-disk bytes are CRLF here and LF in git; a
raw-byte pin holds on exactly one machine. The CRLF trap from memory, closed at the hash — and the
pins were re-verified against the committed bytes after the commit, 15/15.

**Coordinates and namespaces frozen.** Domain-major inside (`states.rs linear_index`,
`P0=0 … O9=39`); the tokenizer's Noetic-major ids `213 + 4·noetic + domain` verified for all 40;
the explicit map lives in the contract. The three 28-way namespaces (Drive×Domain, Drive×Register,
TKS Foundation×World-aspect) are quarantined; no 28-way head in this pilot. Four discrepancy records:
DR-1 the descent mask assumes domain-major slots while the tokenizer is Noetic-major — **open for

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - Helm 2.0's first AWS run died at the FIRST update: non-finite gradients. The forward pass was fine; the backward blew up.

**Job `cas-meta2-tpntr-20260920184534-df20eb46-m-20260917`, machine `i-0e8c342a5ee7819dc`, died about 27
minutes in.** One job reserved `15.00`; real compute was roughly 27 minutes. No machine left running,
cleanup COMPLETE. The study stopped itself (`POINTER_STUDY_STOPPED:INCOMPLETE_JOB`), so seeds 18 and 19
never launched and their reservations were never taken — **only one of the three `15.00` was spent.**

```
cncm/.../pointer_learner.py, line 1938, in train_sealed_batch
    require(all(bool(self.torch.isfinite(parameters[name].grad).all().item())
ValueError: TYPED_LEARNER_GRADIENTS
```

### What the evidence rules out, in order

- **The forward pass was fine.** `failed_seal.json` holds the batch's **8 valid predictions**, with
  `cursor: 0`. The failure is in the BACKWARD, on the **first** update.
- **It is not the ablation blocker.** That is fixed: this run got past preflight, phase A, the
  `course_p00` evaluation, and into pass 1. The 90 artifacts that are missing are missing because the run
  died, not because a check refused.
- **It is not a bad initialisation.** Every tensor's initial scale was measured and is healthy. The three
  tensors Helm 2.0 adds or widens are unremarkable: `match_key.weight` max abs `0.12498`,
  `state_encode.weight` max abs `0.12491`, `reach_option_head.0.weight` max abs `0.06201` — all in line
  with the tensors that were already there.
- **It is not the learning rate.** `failed_seal.json` says `cursor: 0`, so this was the first step.

### MY OWN REASONING WAS WRONG, and it matters

My first instinct was that the unwired F4 warm-up schedule caused it, and I said so. **That cannot be
right.** The failing check is on the raw gradient, and **a gradient does not depend on the learning rate** —
warm-up changes how far the step goes, not whether the gradient is finite. Warm-up may still be worth
wiring, but it is not the fix for this, and I have not wired it on that false premise.

**What the mechanism probably is, stated as a hypothesis and not a finding:** Helm 1.0's loop ran the same
round eight times, so its backward was effectively one round deep. F1 made the loop genuinely iterate, so
gradients now flow back through **eight** rounds of a recurrent update. That is the only structural change
to the backward, and it is exactly the kind of change that turns a surviving run into a diverging one. The
prediction it fits: **Helm 1.0's own seed 17 blew up at pass 8**; Helm 2.0 blew up at update 1.

### What has to happen before another dollar is spent

**A local reproduction, on the RTX 4070, at `$0`.** The tools exist and were checked: the `cas-gpu` venv
has `torch 2.11.0+cu128` with `cuda True` on `NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4070 Laptop GPU`, and `failed_seal.json`
preserves the exact batch that failed. The experiment is to replay that batch against the real learner and
report **which tensor's gradient goes non-finite first** — that names the culprit instead of guessing at it.

**The $0 preflight could never have caught this, and that is the real lesson.** It drives the worker with a
NON-NEURAL stand-in, so it validates control flow, counts, artifacts and phases — and says so in its own
boundary line: *"proves nothing about numerics"*. A numeric blow-up is invisible to it. **A run that
survives the preflight can still die on the first gradient, and this one did.**

**Also required: the worker must be re-frozen under new job names.** `JOB_ALREADY_RESERVED_NO_AUTOMATIC_RETRY`
means this job can never be launched again, so a fix needs a fresh study admission.

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - The ablation fix is PROVEN on the real container, LF damage repaired, and a portability hazard found in the ladder digest.

### The ablation fix, proven before it was written

Simulated against the live Helm 2.0 container, not argued:

```
container tensors      : 48
covered by exactly one : 48
   core_block           3 tensors
   round_mix            4 tensors   (node_encode.weight + state_encode.weight + round_mix + round_rate)
   heads               33 tensors   (+ match_key.)
   query_and_readout    8 tensors
UNCOVERED : none      AMBIGUOUS : none      EMPTY COMP: none
ABLATION_PHASES: UNCHANGED -> EXPECTED_ARTIFACTS stays 99, the evidence contract does not move
```

**Two words in two tuples.** `state_encode.` joins `round_mix` beside `node_encode.weight` (it is the
round's own input projector), and `match_key.` joins `heads` beside `pointer_key.` (it is a scoring
projection). Because no component is ADDED, `ABLATION_PHASES` is identical, so no registered constant
moves and nothing needs re-pinning.

**I also predicted a second blocker and the measurement disproved it.** `_reset_component` line 1438
requires every ablated name to be in `pointer_module._registered_names(...)`, which I expected to hold
only the three `^6` tensors. It holds **all 48**, so `state_encode.weight` and `match_key.weight` both
resolve. Checked rather than assumed.

### Two more run-killers ruled out for free

- **No shared module changed.** Across `git diff 6580add4 HEAD`, only `pointer_learner.py` moved among
  the `typed_teaching` files — `pointer_contract`, `matched_contract`, `practice_contract`,
  `worker_contract`, `learner`, `slots` and `source` are all untouched.
- **`BLOCK_INPUT_WIDTH` is learner-local (192) and is NOT in `contract.DESIGN`.** F1 moved no registered
  constant, so `validate_config` will not reject Helm 2.0 on a config-identity mismatch.

### LF damage: the Helm 2.0 commit converted `CHANGELOG.md` to CRLF

Measured from the git blobs, not the working tree:

| revision | stored as | LF | CRLF |
|---|---|---|---|
| `6580add4` | **LF** | 7176 | **0** |
| `7f3a656f` | **CRLF** | 7239 | 7239 |
| `HEAD` | **CRLF** | 7422 | 7422 |

That is the whole of the 7,239-insertion / 7,176-deletion "change" on a 7,176-line file — a pure line
ending rewrite, no content. The worktree-root `.gitattributes` only pins an LFS zip, so nothing enforced
LF on `CHANGELOG.md`; the subdirectory `.gitattributes` does say `*.md text eol=lf`, which the file
violated. **Repaired:** `CHANGELOG.md` 7422 CRLF lines → LF (649432 → 642010 bytes) and
`HELM_2_0_HANDOFF.md` 185 CRLF lines → LF. Both sit outside `contract.input_inventory`, so neither could
disturb the rehearsal in flight.

### A portability hazard in the ladder, recorded not silently fixed

`ladder_v0_1.json` carries **17,178 CRLF lines** and its digest `4436fb6b481ff606` is computed over those
bytes. The generator writes in Python text mode on Windows, which translates every newline. **So the
README's claim that "two builds produce byte-identical files" holds only on one platform**: regenerate on
Linux and the digest changes. Any result quoting `4436fb6b` must say which platform produced it, and the
generator should write with `newline="\n"` before that digest is treated as portable. **I did not convert
those bytes** — changing them would silently invalidate a digest the ladder publishes, which is worse
than the hazard.

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - TWO INDEPENDENT RULERS AGREE, AND THE BAR IS 0.53 NOT 0.00. The instrument of record is `records/typed_pointer_001/ladder/`.

**Two agents built a difficulty ladder independently, from different constructions, and they agree on the
one thing that matters.** `records/typed_pointer_001/ladder/` (289395 bytes of material, 165 cells,
baselines, a README, and a proof that its worlds are disjoint from everything the study taught or held)
is the **instrument of record**. `records/typed_pointer_001/ruler.py` is a simpler independent build and
now serves as its **cross-check**.

**What they agree on.** A rule with no learned weights that reads one hop of adjacency scores
**1.000 at hop distance 1 and exactly 0.000 at hop distance 2 and beyond**, at every world size. Two
different constructions, same cliff. That is the line Helm 1.0 sat on.

**What the instrument of record adds, and it is sharper than my cross-check:**

| rule | hop 1 | hop ≥ 2 |
|---|---|---|
| `one_hop_adjacency` | 1.000 | **0.000** |
| `rank_last_guess` | 0.000 | **0.534** |
| `constant_answer` | 1.000 | 0.000 |
| **`best_zero_learning`** | **1.000** | **0.534** |
| `true_oracle_ceiling` | 1.000 | 1.000 |

**The bar to beat beyond one hop is about 0.534, not 0.000.** My cross-check put its strongest
mechanism-free rule at 0.375, so the instrument of record is the stricter one and its number is the one
to use. `the_line_to_beat` is recorded in the artifact as `best_zero_learning`.

**And its README states the consequence the audit was reaching for:** because `parts` and
`edge_account` are one-hop tasks that a one-hop rule solves perfectly, **no score on those kinds can
ever be evidence of learning**, however high it goes. That is precisely why Helm 1.0's ten to twelve
gains — all of them on one-answer questions, none on `analogy` — carried no weight. A model also has to
be able to answer "nothing" and not be paid for guessing wrongly.

Both files are additive. Neither reads, writes, or imports the frozen 18 or the pass mark, and
`BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` rule 4 keeps them where they are.

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - F3: THE RULER IS BUILT. A one-hop machine scores 1.0000 at one hop and exactly 0.0000 beyond it, at every size.

**`records/typed_pointer_001/ruler.py`** — the instrument that was missing. `$0`, no GPU, ~2 seconds.
`RULER_SELFTEST_PASS`, 6 checks, exit 0.

### Why the old instrument could not show anything, measured

- **The whole frozen course is 3- and 4-node graphs**: 52 worlds of four nodes, 12 of three, nothing
  larger anywhere. There is no size axis in the material.
- **The development panel is no better.** Its own policy says
  `ALL_64_RETAINED_TRAINING_WORLDS_PLUS_ONE_NONBANK_DEVELOPMENT_FOCUS` — it reuses the same 64 tiny
  worlds and adds 6 bigger focus worlds, and it asks only `parts` and `reach`, with
  `comparison: "Parts/reach development structure-and-size transfer only; not all-eight-kind transfer"`.
- **And an n-node graph can be at most n-1 hops deep.** A four-node world can never be asked a
  four-hop question. That is why Helm 1.0's ceiling could not be exposed: the worlds were too small
  to ask a question deep enough to fail.

### What the ruler does

Three axes: **size** (4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16 nodes), **hops** (1–4, clamped by size because a small
graph cannot be deep), and a **continuous score** reported BESIDE exact match rather than instead of
it. Hop distance is computed by breadth-first search, so the x-axis is **measured, not asserted**.

Depth is **guaranteed, not hoped for**: every extra edge creates a shortcut and shortens the longest
path, so chords are accepted one at a time and only while the depth survives. That bug fired on the
first run and produced a missing cell, which is how the guarantee got written.

Every table prints the lines that matter: `oracle` (breadth-first, the ceiling), **`one_hop` (a
faithful model of Helm 1.0)**, and `rank_last` (the gate's own registered rank rule).

### The measured cliff

| size | hops | oracle exact | **one_hop exact** | rank_last exact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 → 16 | **1** | 1.0000 | **1.0000** | 0.0000 – 0.1429 |
| 4 → 16 | **2** | 1.0000 | **0.0000** | 0.0000 – 0.2222 |
| 4 → 16 | **3** | 1.0000 | **0.0000** | 0.0000 – 0.3333 |
| 5 → 16 | **4** | 1.0000 | **0.0000** | 0.0000 – 0.3750 |

28 cells. Answer pool 133. The one-hop rule is **perfect at one hop and exactly zero beyond it, at
every size** — and the oracle is at 1.0000 in every cell, so the instrument has full headroom to show
a curve. `rank_last` never exceeds 0.3750.

**This is the line Helm 1.0 sat on and could not cross, now drawn on a chart.** On the frozen 18
questions Helm 1.0 looked like it had learned something, because those questions are almost all
one-hop. On this ruler its position would be unambiguous.

**What it does not do:** it does not touch the frozen 18-question set, the grader, or any shared
module. It is additive, and it sits outside `contract.input_inventory`, so it can be written while a
run is in flight. It makes **no claim about Helm** — an instrument is not a measurement. Helm 2.0 has
not been scored on it, and until it is, the ruler proves only that the instrument works.

**The Helm 2.0 entry below is the agent's own report. This entry is my independent verification of it,
plus the consequence for Helm 1.0's record, which that agent correctly left untouched.**

**I confirmed the defect by reading the certified source at `6580add4`.** `_rounds` reads the state into
a local named `block` and never uses it; all three per-round aggregates come from `raw`, the frozen
input rows. The round input is therefore a **constant** and the state drifts the same way eight times.
**The docstring says `norm(h)`. The code says `norm(raw)`.** Reach: one hop, always.

**I ran the repair's own gate myself, on CPU, at `$0`:**

```
records/typed_pointer_001/rounds_iterate_check.py  ->  ROUNDS_ITERATE_PASS, exit 0
tests/test_typed_pointer_study.py -q               ->  22 passed, exit 0
```

Six checks, all real and none vacuous: no assigned-and-never-read name in `_rounds`; round input is
three state vectors (`192 == 3 * 64`); **the round input differs across rounds and grows steadily**
(`0.014739282429218292, 0.02969013899564743, 0.04484092444181442, 0.06017898768186569,
0.07569090276956558, 0.0913623794913292, 0.10717859119176865`); **a change two hops away reaches node
0** (`0.0018496513366699219`, where 1.0 gave exactly `0.000e+00`), with the one-hop neighbourhood
asserted bitwise identical **and** the graph asserted really changed; reach head 260 wide; different
sources prefer different targets with Sinkhorn columns summing to 1 (`1.1920928955078125e-07`).
**174,303 numbers in 48 groups**, matching the claim exactly. The three tensors a 1.0 checkpoint cannot
fill are printed by name, not hidden.

**The consequence for Helm 1.0 — this changes the diagnosis, and it is the important part.** The
certified label stands. What it measured was **the ceiling of a broken loop**:

| the audit found | why |
|---|---|
| a no-learning rule scores **11 of 18**; Helm 12/12/11 | one-hop adjacency **is the ceiling**. A trivial rule was the only thing Helm could compute. |
| `analogy` **0/5** before and after, all seeds | needs cross-world comparison; impossible at one hop. |
| `context`, `definition`, `parts`, `edge_account` **1.00 train and held** | all one-hop, saturated, no headroom. |
| `reach` 0.94 train / 0.88–0.96 held | DIRECT is one hop; **VIA needs two**. |
| `correspondence` **0.09 on its own training data** | every source was scored against the same query vector, so every source got the identical ranking. A one-to-one mapping was **unrepresentable**, not merely unlearned. |
| ten-node panel 7/59, 9/59, 6/59 | bigger graphs put more of the question out of reach. |

**So Helm 1.0's numbers must not be used to argue anything about Helm's capacity. They measure a
defect.** That is now written into `BRAIN_LINEAGE.md`, which also carries a **Helm 2.0** row marked
**NOT TRAINED, NOT CERTIFIED, NO CLAIM** — because building a shape is not a result, and this project
has already been caught once treating a headline as a finding.

**Still gating everything: F3, a ruler.** Eighteen yes/no questions cannot show a learning curve, and
six of them are out of reach for a one-hop machine. Until there is a difficulty ladder by world size and
hop distance plus a continuous score beside exact-match — built **beside** the frozen 18, **never
replacing it** — no scaling run can show anything.

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - HELM 2.0 F1/F2/F4 BUILT. Helm 1.0's eight reasoning rounds were ONE round repeated eight times.

**Branch `research/helm-2.0-iterating-rounds`. Code only. $0. No GPU, no run, no claim promoted,
`BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` untouched.**

### The defect

`pointer_learner.py:_rounds` took its three per-round aggregates from `raw`, the FROZEN node
feature rows, and read the state `hidden` only into a local named `block` that was never used.
So `update` was constant and the recurrence was `h <- rms(h + c*u)` with the SAME `u` eight
times. **The receptive field was exactly ONE hop, at round 8 or at round 800.** The function's
own docstring described the intended recurrence (`norm(h)`); the code did not perform it.

Proven three ways, with the real trained `final.pt`: an AST walk reporting exactly one
assigned-and-never-read name (`block`); all 8 round inputs **bitwise identical**, max|diff|
`0.000e+00`; and a node's final vector **identical** (`0.000e+00`) when the graph changed two
hops away with its one-hop neighbourhood held fixed.

This explains every Helm 1.0 reading: `context`/`definition`/`parts`/`edge_account` at 1.00
train AND 1.00 held (saturated one-hop tasks, no headroom); `reach` 0.94 (DIRECT is one hop,
VIA needs two); `analogy` 0.42 train / **0.00 held**; the ten-node panel at `7/59, 9/59, 6/59`.
And it explains the audit's floor result: **the one-hop adjacency rule IS the architecture's
ceiling**, so Helm 1.0 converged onto the trivial baseline because that is what it could compute.

### Built

- **F1** the loop reads its own state; new `state_encode` `Linear(64,64)`; `BLOCK_INPUT_WIDTH`
  54 -> 192. Receptive field 1 hop -> `CORE_ROUNDS` = 8.
- **F2a** the `reach` ABSENT/DIRECT head is shown the query's node pair. It previously decided
  "is this part inside that whole" **without being shown either node**; `reach` carries 6 of the
  18 gate questions. Head input `2*WIDTH+4` -> `4*WIDTH+4`.
- **F2b** `correspondence` gets a bilinear pair score (`match_key`) plus 3 log-domain Sinkhorn
  steps. Helm 1.0 scored every `mapping_target` slot against `pointer_query`, which depends on
  the QUERY and not the SOURCE, so **every source node got the identical ranking over targets**
  and a bijection was UNREPRESENTABLE. That is why it sat at 0.09 on its own training data.
- **F4** gradient clipping at global norm 1.0, plus an opt-in warm-up/cosine schedule. Both are
  RECORDED in every update row (`gradient_norm_before_clip`, `gradient_was_clipped`,
  `learning_rate_this_step`, `learning_rate_schedule`). Motivation: seed 20260917 went grad^2
  0.622 -> 19.159 -> 28.822 across passes 7-9 with the loss RISING, on a constant 0.003 rate with
  no clipping; that is the seed whose `role_set` finished at 0.13 while seed 20260918 reached 1.00.
  **The schedule defaults OFF** -- it activates only when a caller puts `planned_main_updates` in
  `limits`, so an existing run's arithmetic is bitwise unchanged unless it opts in.

Brain: **157,919 numbers in 46 groups -> 174,303 in 48. +16,384, +10.4%.**

### Gate

`records/typed_pointer_001/rounds_iterate_check.py` -- 6 checks, `$0`, ~10 s, no CUDA. Prints
`ROUNDS_ITERATE_PASS`. Round-to-round difference now GROWS monotonically (0.034 -> 0.230), which
is information spreading one hop per round; the two-hop test moved off `0.000e+00`.
**Nothing should be launched if this does not pass.**

### NOT done -- next agent picks up at `records/typed_pointer_001/HELM_2_0_HANDOFF.md`

Wire `planned_main_updates` through the worker (the schedule is dormant without it); held-back
loss per pass; multi-head attention (`attention_key` is 64 numbers today); and **F3, the big one**
-- a difficulty ladder and a continuous score, built BESIDE the frozen 18 and never replacing it,
because 18 yes/no questions cannot show a curve. F5 scaling comes after F3, `CORE_ROUNDS` first
because the round is shared and costs zero new parameters.

**No result is claimed. The machine is now CAPABLE of things Helm 1.0 could not represent.
Whether it does them is a question for a run and for the frozen grader, which is untouched.**

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - INDEPENDENT AUDIT DOWNGRADES HELM 1.0's INTERPRETATION. A rule that learns nothing scores 11 of 18 on the label's own questions.

**The label stands. My interpretation of it does not.** An independent audit
(`records/typed_pointer_001/AUDIT_REPORT_20260920_INDEPENDENT.md`, 22221 bytes, sha256
`9d8826c5c09689f9a65323f5b31f070641beb52c0911814c4174d43322ba60fb`) reproduced every number I
published, then found two things I had left out and one thing I had got wrong.

### 1. The zero-learning floor — the certificate said it and I did not

The grader's own certifying file carries an `oracle_ceilings` block. I built `BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` from
the headline numbers and **never read that block.** It says:

```
oracle_or_rank_solvable_in_the_label_population: 11     (of the 18 the label is keyed on)
oracle_solvable_in_the_label_population: 7              (one-hop read: edge_account 5 + parts 2)
applied_as_removals: false
floor_note: "a zero-learning rule already answers these questions of the label's own population;
             they are REPORTED and NOT subtracted, so the label must be read beside them"
```

**Helm scored 12, 12, 11. A hard-coded rule that learns nothing scores 11.** Correct answers Helm
produced that the no-learning rule does not: **2, 2, 2**. Run the gate's OWN bar (improved ≥ 7,
broken 0) against that rule instead of against the untrained machine, and **all three seeds fail**:
p = 0.50, 0.50, 0.69.

**And it scores 0 of 5 on `analogy` — the only one of the four kinds in the 18 that no trivial rule
can touch — before and after, in all three seeds.** Independently verified here: **all 33 gains sit
on the 13 questions with exactly one acceptable answer; zero gains on the 5 with more than one.**

So the honest headline is **+2 over a program that reads the links off the page, at even odds** — not
`0–2 → 11–12 of 18` at 1-in-1024. Both are true; the second is measured against a starting point that
cannot read the page. Helm 1.0 learned to apply a one-hop structural rule to structures it had never
seen. It did **not** demonstrate the broader structural comparison the study was built to test, and
the certified label cannot tell those two apart.

### 2. The certificate is not bound to the evidence — a forgery path, and a new check for it

The label is a function of two editable JSON files per seed. **The grader never checks them against
the sha256 the run itself recorded in `artifact_manifest.json`** — its only byte-level digest
comparison is the practice start-checkpoint pin at `evaluate_gates.py:646`. The auditor proved it by
hand-editing seed `20260919`'s `course_p10_scoring.json` so every real-correct answer read wrong and
every real-wrong read right: the grader still printed `LEARNS_NEW_WORLDS` with
`integrity: pass, failures: []`.

**The real files are not forged.** Verified here independently: **297 of 297 evidence files across
the three receipts match their own recorded hashes, 0 mismatched, 0 missing.** But the guarantee was
weaker than this project claimed.

**Added `records/typed_pointer_001/verify_evidence_hashes.py`** — the missing check, deliberately
*beside* the frozen grader rather than inside it, because editing `evaluate_gates.py` would move its
sha256 and the certificate `475379d2...` could no longer be reproduced. It runs `EVIDENCE_HASH_CHECK:
PASS` on the real evidence and **catches a tampered copy** (proved on a scratch copy of seed 18 with
every `correct` flag flipped: exit 1, both hashes printed). `BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` rule 3 now names the
two scoring files, and rule 4 requires reading the `oracle_ceilings` block.

### 3. Two wording corrections I had got wrong

- **"new wiring" is false.** All 5 held-out worlds reuse a link pattern teaching already contained.
  Already corrected earlier today.
- **"names it had never seen" is not quite right either.** Two of the 20 part names in the test worlds
  — `Chuck` and `Gear` — were taught. Corrected in `BRAIN_LINEAGE.md`.
- Also fixed: `AUDIT_PACK.md` check 4 said "empty scratch dir"; the script needs a path that does not
  exist, or it stops with `FileExistsError`.

### What the audit confirmed

Every hash matched; self-test `SELFTEST_PASS 50 checks`; hold-out `HOLDOUT_AUDIT_PASS`; `22 passed`;
**the reading re-ran byte-for-byte identical** to the committed certificate (202862 bytes,
`475379d2...`); every published number recomputed exactly from raw `correct` flags; 157,919 numbers in
46 groups, `declaration_table` 41.50%; zero leakage, 358/358 trained ids updated; seed identities
reproducible across all three machines. **The commonest-answer guessing attack scores 0 of 45 — that
attack is dead.**

`BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` now leads the Helm 1.0 block with the floor, not with the headline.

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - MEASURED WEAKNESS IN HELM'S TEST: all five "never-shown worlds" reuse wiring that teaching already contained.

**A directive document told me to check for near-duplicates in a train/test split. I ran the check, and
Helm's split is weaker than I had been describing.**

Throw the part names away and compare only the wiring — which part points at which — treating every
renaming of the same pattern as the same pattern. Measured on the frozen course:

- Teaching used **19 worlds but only 6 distinct wiring patterns**. The counts are 5, 5, 3, 3, 2 and 1.
- The five test worlds use **4 distinct patterns**, and **all 4 are among those 6**.
- Therefore **5 of 5 held-out worlds** are a pattern Helm had already practised, wearing new part
  names.
- Part names ARE genuinely new: 68 distinct names in teaching, 20 in the test worlds, and only
  **two** overlap — `Chuck` and `Gear`.

**What this does not change.** The certified label stands. The grader is frozen, the question set was
fixed before any result was seen, and those five worlds genuinely were never read in training.
`LEARNS_NEW_WORLDS` was printed by that grader and is not withdrawn.

**What it does change: the meaning of the words.** The honest description of Helm 1.0 is **new parts,
new names, already-seen wiring** — not "new worlds" as a reader would assume. Helm did not have to work
out a new kind of structure. It had to apply a structure it had seen before to parts it had not, and
get the direction right. That is still real work — the before-behaviour was listing every possible
pair and naming a part as containing itself — but the test is easier than the headline suggests.

**This is exactly the failure mode the directive names** (`Single arithmetic template trivializes the
test → Add structurally distinct supported tasks`; `Group descendants and near-duplicate worlds into
one split`). It is the first concrete thing obtained from reading that document, and it is a
weakening, not a strengthening.

**Fix for Helm 1.1:** held-out wiring patterns that appear **nowhere** in teaching, and worlds with
more than four parts. Six patterns is a thin basis for a transfer claim, and the present test cannot
detect whether Helm can handle a shape it has never seen.

**Also recorded from the same document:** Helm's result is on a **fixed, stored** test set, so by that
document's own section 12 it is a *screening* result. "Fresh benefit" — does it help on genuinely new
work under matched conditions — has **not** been demonstrated for Helm.

`BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` carries the measurement, a corrected "may not claim" list, and the recommended fix.

### Every gate, and the three that did not pass

Passed, all three seeds: `integrity` (`failures: []`), **`G_LEARN_population`** (the label's own

Did not pass, all three seeds, and **none of them is the label**:

- **`MORE_PRACTICE_population` — failed.** It asks whether practice *beyond the first pass* bought
  anything on the frozen 18. It did not; most of the gain lands in pass 1. So the honest reading of
  Helm 1.0 is "it learned from the teaching it was given", **not** "it kept improving the longer it
  trained". This agrees with the earlier finding that the loss flattened from pass 6.
- **`G_LEARN_TEXT` — failed.** That is the `role_set` tier, 6 questions: **0 gained, 0 lost**. A null
  result on a tier the label does not use.
- **`kind_g_learn` — all eight failed.** Each compares one kind alone, and the small kinds hold only 5
  or 6 questions. With a bar of 7 improved, a 5-question kind **cannot pass by construction**. These
  are informational; the real gains are in `edge_account`, `parts` and `reach`, which are exactly the
  three kinds the primary test names as improved.

### What may and may not be claimed

**MAY:** Helm 1.0 learned to read which part contains which in machines it had never been shown,
measured by a frozen grader across three independent runs with the question set fixed in advance.

**MAY NOT:** that it generalises beyond small structures of parts and containment links; that the
transfer crosses into a novel *domain* — the five held-out worlds are the same kind of thing as 11 of
the taught ones, machines, so this is new individuals with new names and new wiring; that extra
practice beyond pass 1 helps; that the whole CNCM machine exists.

**Certified is not the same as independently audited.** The grader is frozen and the runs are real,
but one session built the machine and read the result. `AUDIT_PACK.md` lists what an outside agent
should run and the eight attempts to break this claim.

`BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` now carries **Helm 1.0** as the current version with the certified row, and keeps
**Helm 0.1**'s uncertified record beneath it, unedited. Cost: three jobs at `15.00`, free
`4259.00` of `5000.00`. No machine left running.

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - The learning brain is NAMED. It is **Helm**, it is versioned, and there is now one file that cannot lose it.

**The brain that learns is `Helm`, current version `0.1`.** The owner chose the name. Everything about
it that must survive being handed to another agent now lives in ONE file at the CNCM homebase root:

`BRAIN_LINEAGE.md`

It carries the name and version, the versioning scheme, the number of learned numbers, the worlds it
was taught on, the worlds it was tested on and never shown, every measured score with the before and
after, the official verdict, the exact evidence folders, a generations table, and six rules for
whoever comes next. `AGENTS.md` now points at it from a new "The learning brain" section, so an agent
working in this homebase meets it before it meets the pointer code.

**The scheme, so a milestone is never a surprise:** `0.x` = being tried out, nothing certified.
`1.0` = the first version that passes the official three-run test. `1.1, 1.2` = same shape, trained
better. `2.0` = the shape changed, so the number count moved.

**Size, measured, because "will the parameters grow" has a real answer.** Helm 0.1 holds **157,919**
learned numbers in **46** groups. The largest single group is the word table `declaration_table`,
1,024 × 64 = **65,536**, which is **41.5%** of the brain. Learning changes these values and never the
count: it does not grow by getting smarter. It grows only when the working width is widened on
purpose, which roughly quadruples most groups when the width doubles and would take the count to
about 630,000 — and that is a version bump, not an edit.

**What the teaching actually was, for the record.** 19 worlds: **11 machines** (four parts, three
links each) and **8 grammar patterns** (e.g. "a list pattern": List, Item, Determiner, Marker).
8 question kinds, 564 questions, 358 practised, 127 held back, 79 set aside. Trained 10 passes,
1470 updates, about 526 s per pass.

**And the five worlds it was never shown, in full**, all machines in a workshop:

| machine | its parts | what contains what |
|---|---|---|
| a weaving machine | Bearing, Loom, Shuttle, Thread | Loom→Thread, Shuttle→Thread, Loom→Bearing |
| a turning machine | Bolt, Chuck, Lathe, Tool | Lathe→Chuck, Lathe→Tool, Tool→Bolt |
| a transmission assembly | Gear, Gearbox, Manifold, Shaft | Gearbox→Shaft, Shaft→Gear, Gear→Manifold |
| a stopping assembly | Brake, Disc, Pad, Pinion | Brake→Pad, Brake→Disc, Disc→Pinion |
| a lifting machine | Bracket, Cable, Crane, Hook | Crane→Hook, Crane→Cable, Cable→Bracket, Hook→Bracket |

**Scope stated honestly, because it would be easy to overstate.** These five are the SAME KIND of thing
as 11 of the worlds it was taught — machines. So this is transfer to **new individuals with new part
names and new wiring**, not to a wholly new kind of thing. Real transfer, which a memorising machine
would fail. Not a claim about novel domains.

**The four clearest examples, in the machine's own words**, all on worlds it had never seen:

| the machine | before | after | correct |
|---|---|---|---|
| a turning machine, every link | 7 links incl. `Tool contains Tool`, `Bolt contains Lathe` | `Lathe→Chuck`, `Lathe→Tool`, `Tool→Bolt` | same |
| a weaving machine, every link | all **12** possible pairs incl. `Thread contains Thread` | `Loom→Bearing`, `Loom→Thread`, `Shuttle→Thread` | same |
| a weaving machine, what the Loom holds | **nothing** — empty answer | Bearing and Thread | Bearing and Thread |
| a transmission assembly, what the Gearbox holds | Manifold | Shaft | Shaft |

Before, it was guessing: every pair, a part containing itself, or nothing. After, it read the structure.

**Official verdict, unchanged and prominent: NOT CERTIFIED.** Helm 0.1's learning is measured with the
graders' own test code on a question set fixed before the result was known — 2/18 → 12/18, gained 10,
lost 0, bar 7, odds 1 in 1024 — and no grader program has certified it, because that run's certificate
can never exist. `BRAIN_LINEAGE.md` states this as the most important row in the file and forbids
writing "passed" about it.

because `receipt_20260917/result.json` does not exist and cannot legitimately exist. That file is not the
worker's — it is the **launcher lifecycle's own record** (`schema: cas-graph-training-candidate-launch/1`, carrying
`completed_deliverable`, `cleanup`, `stages`, `reservation`, `ledger_before`/`ledger_after`), and the gate reads
`completed_deliverable` and `cleanup.complete` out of it. The launcher died before writing it. Two hard reasons it
cannot be recovered:

1. **`graph_training_curriculum_launch.py:1622-1624` refuses a job already in the ledger** —
   `JOB_ALREADY_RESERVED_NO_AUTOMATIC_RETRY`. Job `...-m-20260917` is permanently single-shot; it can never be
   launched again, and its `15.00` reservation will not return (`no_automatic_refund: true`).
2. **There is no collection-only entry point.** `provider.collect()` exists, but the lifecycle record is written
   inside `lifecycle.execute()` after a fresh `provider.reserve()` that the check above refuses. Writing
   `result.json` by hand would be manufacturing the exact lifecycle record the gate trusts, so I did not.

The evidence is therefore kept as `records/typed_pointer_001/receipt_20260917_orphaned_1af25973/` (the launcher's
own `evidence_contract.json`, `intent.json`, `preflight.json`, `reservation.json`, `launch.json` and its
`provider_*.json` records), and the S3 objects for bucket
`cas-meta2-tpntr-20260920033528-1af25973-m-20260917-807562639912` are the only copy of the worker's records. The
instance `i-03f50a5d5c7e6da44` was terminated by me after the worker finished, so billing stopped; the other
workstream's `i-0c3028734c8885542` (`cas-bridge-control-20260822`) was not touched.

**The lesson, stated plainly: one driver process for three single-shot jobs is a single point of failure, and the
cost of that failure is not the broken job — it is a fully successful run whose evidence no gate will ever accept.**
The recovery is a fresh study admission, which produces three NEW job names the ledger will accept, launched
detached.

**Cost of my mistake, exact.** The lost seed-17 job reserved `15.00` and its real compute was

## 2026-09-20 UTC (POINTER) - The eighth run died in 90 seconds on a function that has never existed. Fixing the carrier is what executed that branch for the first time.

**Shrinking the smoke schedule was never possible, so the code that shrinks it was never run.** Job
`cas-meta2-tpntr-20260920023737-2010c4b6-m-20260918`, receipt `records/typed_pointer_001/receipt_smoke_20260918`,
`status: AWS_CANDIDATE_FAILED`, `execution: FAILED`, died before any training or evaluation file existed:

```
File "/opt/cas-graph6curr/package/cncm/typed_pointer_worker.py", line 357, in _schedule
    counts = matched._slot_counts(frozen)
AttributeError: module 'cncm.typed_matched_worker' has no attribute '_slot_counts'
```

**A one-word module-alias error, and the alias itself is correct everywhere else.** `_slot_counts` lives in
`cas_meta2/typed_teaching/matched_contract.py:78`. `pointer_contract.py:74` binds `matched_contract as matched`
and its own call at line 312 works. `cncm/typed_pointer_worker.py:123` binds
`from . import typed_matched_worker as matched` — a DIFFERENT module — and line 357 assumed it carried
`_slot_counts`. The alias is load-bearing and must NOT be repointed: `worker.matched._framework` is used at lines
789, 855, 1137, 1264 and `worker.matched._sum_deltas` at 1061, 1100, 1788. So the call was repointed instead, to
`matched_contract._slot_counts`, via an explicit import that keeps the worker's recomputation on exactly the
function the contract already used.

**Why 1,470 updates never touched it.** `contract.pointer_schedule` computes `counts` itself from that same
function and returns complete `stats`, so the registered path reads its slot totals from the contract and never
needed the worker's copy. That copy exists only to recompute the three slot totals for the TRUNCATED batch list,
and the truncated branch is guarded by `plan["updates"] < stats["main_updates"]` — unreachable while the shrunken
schedule could not reach an instance. The carrier fix is what made it reachable. **This is the cost of a fix that
was verified against a render instead of against the artifact, and it is the second defect the carrier bug was
hiding.**

**Verified for `$0` on the frozen material, no torch, no AWS** — and the strong check is a CROSS-CHECK that the
worker's recomputed totals equal the totals the contract itself produced, so a second drifting copy cannot pass:

| check | result |
|---|---|
| `matched_contract._slot_counts` exists | yes |
| the old target `typed_matched_worker` has `_slot_counts` | **no** — the defect was real |
| the alias still carries `_framework` and `_sum_deltas` | yes, both |
| registered plan through the worker's own `_schedule` | 1470 batches, main_updates 1470 |
| worker's own counts over ALL batches vs `presentation_slots` | **56510 == 56510** |
| worker's own counts over ALL batches vs `planned_slot_core_passes` | **212080 == 212080** |
| shrunken plan | 8 batches, main_updates 8, `reduced_updates: True` |
| shrunken `presentation_slots` vs an independent recount | 304 == 304 |
| smoke plan artifact names | 40 (matches the admission record) |

`REDUCED BRANCH VERIFICATION: PASSED`. Two of my own assertions in that script were wrong before they were right,
and both were the same mistake: I asserted `presentation_slots == 32280` and `planned_slot_core_passes == 124510`
from a comment, and `pointer_contract.py:436-440` says in as many words that those are the PRACTICE study's
old-machine numbers "that this machine does not have". The real pointer numbers are 56510 and 212080, and the
check is now a NEGATIVE assertion as well, so the two pairs cannot be confused again.

`tests/test_typed_pointer_study.py`: 22 passed (114.81 s).

**The headline, measured.** The pointer machine's neurons learned a skill that transfers to worlds they were never
shown. On the frozen gate's own 18-question RELATIONAL population, the seventh run's own scoring files
(`course_p00_scoring.json` at cursor 0, `course_p10_scoring.json` at cursor 1470) give:

| gate quantity | required | measured |
|---|---|---|
| `wrong_to_right` (b) | `MIN_PASSING_B` = 7 | **10** |
| `right_to_wrong` (c) | 0 | **0** |
| questions correct, population | — | **2 of 18 → 12 of 18** |
| improved kinds | 2 | **3** (`edge_account`, `parts`, `reach`) |
| improved worlds | 2 | **5** |
| `p_one_sided` | — | **1/1024** = 0.0009765625 |

`label_key_divergence_failures` is `[]`, so the test ran over exactly the frozen population. Side tests:
`ALL_KINDS` n=45 b=22 c=0 passed; `TIER RELATIONAL` n=29 b=12 c=0; `TIER DIRECT_READ` n=10 b=10 c=0;
`TRAINED` start→final n=189 b=127 c=1. The gate's own `worker_contract_failures`,
`schedule_plan_failures` and `min_passing_b_failures` all return `[]` on this receipt.

**CLAIM STATUS, STATED AS I FOUND IT.** The gate's *arithmetic* passes. **The gate program did not print a label
and I do not claim one.** The run's own record says `status: TYPED_POINTER_FAILED`, `completed_deliverable:
False`, 25 of its 99 required artifacts absent, and only 1 of the 3 required seeds exists. So the reading is a
refusal, correctly. This is a MEASURED learning signal, not a gate pass.

**I HAD MISLABELLED THE RUN, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS.** I had been calling
`records/typed_pointer_001/receipt_smoke_20260919f` a reduced "smoke" run. It is not. `worker_report.json` and
`measurements.json` both say `claim_bearing: true`, `claimed_bearing_run: true`, `schedule_plan_mode: REGISTERED`,
`required_artifacts` = 99, `pass_summaries` = 10 passes, `main_training_delta.optimizer_updates` = 1470. It is the
FULL registered 10-pass run. The folder name and a stale `reduced_schedule.json` beside it misled me.

**Root cause of the failure, measured rather than argued.** `_fingerprint` guarded with `type(value) is dict`, but
`nn.Module.state_dict()` returns an `OrderedDict`. The SAVED side is a genuine `dict` (`export_checkpoint` saves
`dict(self.module.state_dict())`) and fingerprinted as 46 rows; the LIVE side fingerprinted as `()`. `()` never
equals a 46-row tuple, so the reload refused on a container that matched the checkpoint EXACTLY — on every reload,
after 1,470 updates had been paid for. Nothing raised on the way in, because the empty tuple is the same answer
the helper documents for "owns no tensors". The gate is now `isinstance(value, Mapping)`, and the empty answer is
checked separately at the call site so the two meanings cannot share one expression again.

**Verified for `$0` before any further spend**, by the CPU harness against the failed run's OWN checkpoints:

```
live container state_dict type: OrderedDict   rows read by _fingerprint: 46 (plain dict copy: 46)
PHASE E PASSED: export -> restore round trip is exact      (46 params, bitwise equal)
stale_layout_refused: True   stale_layout_refusal: POINTER_CHECKPOINT_LAYOUT   <- negative control, not vacuous
initial.pt pass_01.pt prelast.pt final.pt retained.pt  ->  saved=46 live=46 layouts_equal=True differing=0 strict_load=True
```

The five real checkpoints that killed the seventh run now restore exactly. The refusal sat AFTER the run-identity
and profile checks, and the failure was `POINTER_CHECKPOINT_LAYOUT` rather than `TYPED_CHECKPOINT_RUN_IDENTITY`,
so the profile check had already passed on the instance: this type test was the only thing standing between that
run and a complete artifact set. `SELFTEST_PASS 50 checks`, `sha256
d3e0ea979835fa548ae4fe4081280839b5b629296f24735a70e90e8880cdc90d` — the frozen gate is byte-identical.

**The 25 missing artifacts were exactly the steps after the reload** — 10 ablation files + `ablations.json` +
`checkpoint_check_phase_b.json`, 3 reload prediction files + 7 `replay_*`, `installed_sources.json` +
`loaded_sources.json`, and `phase_timings.json` (= 25). One bug, 25 consequences.

**A second defect, and my previous "fix" for it was verified against the wrong artifact.** The shrunken smoke
schedule crossed the process boundary as one line that `bootstrap_source()` inserts into the generated bootstrap,
and that bootstrap is a MEMBER of `source.zip`. `prepare_smoke.py` set the opt-in only AFTER building the package
and then rendered the bootstrap in-process to "prove the smoke's schedule reaches the instance" — which proves
nothing about the bytes that ship. Consequences, all read out of the seventh run's receipt: the smoke package was
byte-identical to the claim-bearing one, the instance ran the full registered plan, and the launcher graded it
against the 43-artifact smoke contract (`collection: INCOMPLETE`, 31 of 43), while the worker reported
`claim_bearing: true` with 99 required. The opt-in is now set BEFORE the build, and
`verify_bootstrap_embedding(package)` reads the line out of the built `source.zip` itself. The claim-bearing
package is built by `prepare_study.py` in a process whose opt-in is `None`, so no pinned package byte moves.

**Both fixes verified for `$0`, and the carrier fix verified IN THE SHIPPED BYTES.** `prepare_smoke.py --verify`
is not a thing, so the proof is a local script that builds two packages from the same clean HEAD differing only in
the opt-in. `SMOKE CARRIER PROOF: PASSED`, six of six:

| check | result |
|---|---|
| smoke package carries the env line, in the shipped `source.zip` | **exactly once** |
| claim-bearing package carries the env line | **0 times** |
| the two packages differ | `1e146f59629df549` vs `813acb9da8519be5` |
| smoke package is the archived package that shipped the full plan | **no** |
| `verify_bootstrap_embedding` on the built smoke package | **accepted** |
| `verify_bootstrap_embedding` on the claim-bearing package | **rejected** (`SMOKE_PACKAGE_ENV_VAR_COUNT:0`) |

The shipped member reads, on adjacent positions:

```
os.environ['CAS_META2_RUN_TEACHING_CYCLE'] = '1'
os.environ['CNCM_POINTER_REDUCED_SCHEDULE'] = '{"ablations":false,...,"passes":1,...,"updates":8}'
from cncm import typed_pointer_worker
```

**Three of my own checks were wrong before they were right, and each wrongness was measured rather than
reasoned away.** The bootstrap is embedded as a ONE-LINE string literal, so (1) `launch.BOOTSTRAP_ENV_ANCHOR` is
not a substring of the member at all — `ValueError: substring not found` on a *correct* package; (2) newlines
arrive as the two characters `\` + `n`, so walking to the next real newline walked from offset 3144 straight past
the env var at 3198 to 3695; (3) the text immediately before the variable name is `os.environ[\'`, 13 characters,
because the quote is escaped too, so subtracting `len('os.environ[')` landed two characters short. The check now
locates the anchor by its distinctive token, measures the gap to the start of the assignment by a BACKWARD search,
and asserts exactly one separator, no intervening `os.environ[`, and a gap of at most 48 characters.

**Admission state.** `prepare_study.py` writes `records/typed_pointer_001/admission_001`, which does NOT exist:
the claim-bearing admission has never been frozen. The six `receipt_smoke_*` directories are the six failed
launches. The re-freeze is required now because `pointer_learner.py` is under `research/` and therefore IS a
package member, so the fix moves the pinned `archive_sha256`/`manifest_sha256`. `prepare_smoke.py` is under
`records/` and is NOT a member (`git_package_bytes` permits only `research/`, `contract.MATERIAL`, and
`cncm/*.py`), so its edits move no pinned package byte.

**CORRECTION TO THE GOAL'S PREMISE.** The goal states the last run "stopped at pass 10 with the loss still
falling" and that training length is a live lever. The loss CONVERGED. Per-pass mean raw loss: 0.9967, 0.7020,

**Money, read live from `_worktrees/meta1/.../campaign_ledger.json` at revision 94**: 7 pointer jobs × `15.00` =
