## 2026-09-19 UTC (GPU RUNS) - Five runs, five distinct bugs, each one further along. The pattern is hand-written width arithmetic.

**Each run advanced strictly further than the last and died on a different defect.** That is progress bought at
roughly `$1.20` per attempt, and it is not a good way to buy it. The pattern is now unmistakable and is the most
useful thing this sequence produced.

| run | died at | defect | fixed |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | container line 12 | `MANIFEST['inputs_sha256'] != CONFIG['inputs_sha256']` — the manifest carried the enriched inventory rows, the config the bare ones | `3ee1be8c`, `1c7054d2` |
| 2 | first training forward | `node_head` built for `NODE_FEATURE_COUNT + WIDTH` = 72, fed `hidden + query` = 128 | `75bd46d2` |
| 3 | first world scoring | `WORLD_CANDIDATE_WIDTH` declared `2 * WIDTH + 4 + DECL`, but the candidate is two **dot products** = `2 + 4 + DECL` | `0e5d1c98` |
| 4 | first prediction | `_logit_rows(torch, plan, symbols, rows)` — `symbols` was **never read**; every caller passed three arguments | (this sequence) |
| 5 | mode-option scoring | `_mode_options` reshapes to `2 * WIDTH + 2 * WIDTH` = 256 while concatenating three `WIDTH`-wide tensors = 192 | **NOT YET FIXED** |

### The systemic defect, stated once so it is fixed as a class rather than five times

**Every one of these is the same failure: a width written out by hand in one place drifting away from the tensors
actually constructed in another.** Four of the five are literally that. And the runtime guards do not catch them,
because each guard compares the tensor to **the same hand-written constant the head was built from** — so the two
agree with each other while both disagree with reality. That is exactly how the guard I myself added for the node
head passed while the head could not accept its input.

**The fix that would end this class**, and it should be done before the next paid run:

1. **Derive every reshape from the tensor, not from arithmetic.** `.reshape(1, -1)` then check the result against
   the consuming module's own `in_features`, rather than writing `2 * WIDTH + 2 * WIDTH` by hand.
2. **Assert against the module, never against a restatement of the input.** The check that works is
   `require(int(head[0].in_features) == int(tensor.shape[1]))`. Anything that recomputes the expected width from
   the same constants the head used is decoration.
3. **Add one test that instantiates the container on CPU and drives a single `predict` on a synthetic packet.**
   The existing suite is green at 22 passed and catches none of these, because nothing in it scores a world or
   asserts a head's `in_features`. One such test would have caught all five for `$0`.

**A local CPU harness is possible and is the highest-value next step.** `_build_container(torch, framework, device)`
takes a device, and every defect above is a pure shape error that needs no GPU, no training and no special
hardware — the AWS instance was never required to find any of them.

### Money, honest

Five paid attempts. Cash roughly `$1.62` for the first four plus about `$1.20` for the fifth: **≈ `$3.00` total.**
Cap commitments: **one `$15.00` reservation per attempt**, with no refund path. Free cap is reported by the
ledger, not by memory. The instance was cleaned up on every run (`compute_cleanup: COMPLETE`) and nothing is left
running.

### What is genuinely proven on real hardware now

`model_identity` from the runs: `core_identity_equals_the_practice_start: true`, `double_build_equal: true`,
`seeds_pairwise_different: true`, `phase_a_finished_before_the_model_was_seeded: true`,
`phase_a_status: COMPLETE`, `whole_container_identity != core_identity`, on
`NVIDIA RTX PRO 4500 Blackwell Server Edition`, `torch 2.11.0+cu128`, `cuda:0`. The machine builds reproducibly,
matches the practice study's pinned start **across machines**, runs the checkpoint check to completion, measures
its own custody ordering, and reports its degraded checks honestly. The pipeline is real; what is left is shape
arithmetic in one module.

**The neurons have still never completed a training update.** Every run died inside the first evaluation.

## 2026-09-19 UTC ($0) - ROOT CAUSE of the smoke failure: M3's fix changed the row SHAPE, and `make_config` still computes the old one

**Diagnosed for $0, without re-spending, and it corrects the hypothesis I committed one round earlier.**

I had written that the failure was probably caused by my committing the launcher's `--reduced-schedule` fix *after*
the freeze. **That was wrong**, and checking it cost nothing instead of another ~$0.40 and a second $15 commitment.
A frozen admission is internally consistent; the two hashes disagree inside **one** freeze.

### The three hashes, read straight out of the frozen artifacts

```
AUTHORITY inputs_sha256                : ff5820e6f0a208fc0916878f9155d3747802102275b57ac4fb13baf59da395e4
MANIFEST  inputs_sha256 (in the package): ff5820e6f0a208fc0916878f9155d3747802102275b57ac4fb13baf59da395e4   <-- MATCH
AUTHORITY worker_config.inputs_sha256  : 7927529ebdcb6aaee0b916be3e9c97aaeeb6268efeee286bbad791c61845d377   <-- DIFFERENT
```

`_inner_runner.py:12` compares **MANIFEST against CONFIG**. The authority's own `inputs_sha256` and the package
manifest agree with each other; it is the **config's** that stands apart.

### The mechanism

- `worker_config(job, *, source_sha)` (`typed_pointer_launch.py:368-371`) delegates: it calls
  **`contract.make_config(PROJECT, source_sha=..., job=..., model_seed=..., bucket=...)`**.
- So the config's `inputs_sha256` is computed **inside `pointer_contract`**, from `contract.input_inventory`.
- M3's fix gave the launcher's own rows a shape change — it **adds a `committed_content_sha256` field to every
  row** and sources them from `git ls-tree`/`git show` at the commit.
- `frozen_inputs_hash()` and the package manifest hash **the launcher's rows**; `make_config` hashes
  **`contract.input_inventory`'s rows**. After M3 those are **different dicts**, so
  `digest(canonical(rows))` differs **by construction** — not because the tree was dirty, and not because anything
  changed between the freeze and the launch.

**So `validate_authority` passed** (it compares the authority's hash to `frozen_inputs_hash`, both the new shape)
while the worker's bootstrap correctly refused (it compares the manifest to the **config**, which is the old
shape). Two internal checks, each self-consistent, disagreeing with each other. **Nothing tested that the config's
hash and the launcher's hash are the same number** — and that is the missing guard.

### The fix

Make the two agree on **one** computation. The minimal, safest option: have `frozen_inputs_hash()` compute its
digest over rows in `contract.input_inventory`'s shape — i.e. strip the added `committed_content_sha256` before
hashing, or hash the same projection `make_config` uses — so the added field remains available as evidence without
changing the number that is compared. The alternative (make `make_config` read the commit) is a change to a
**shared contract module**, which this study's standing rule forbids, so it is not available.

**A guard belongs with it:** assert at FREEZE time that `authority["worker_config"]["inputs_sha256"] ==
authority["inputs_sha256"] == frozen_inputs_hash(source_sha)`, so a future divergence of this shape is caught for
**$0** instead of on an instance.

### Cost of the lesson

~$0.40 cash and a $15.00 cap commitment, and **the neurons were never trained** — the container refused at line 12,
before the encoder, before phase A. The smoke is otherwise proven to the bootstrap on real infrastructure.

## 2026-09-19 UTC (FIRST REAL SPEND) - The smoke launched and failed at the container's first check; ~$0.40 cash, $15.00 cap committed

**This is the first money spent on this study.** It bought a real defect found on real infrastructure. It did **not**
train anything.

### What ran

The smoke job launched for real at `12:59:07` through `typed_pointer_launch.py --execute` with the seven required
arguments plus the new `--reduced-schedule <literal>`, against the frozen admission
`records/typed_pointer_001/admission_smoke_001/` and the canonical `meta1` ledger. It cleared validation, took the
reservation, created a `g7.2xlarge` in `us-west-2` (AMI `ami-0fbbd0facbc9e3cff`, observed rate `2.52`/h,
`gpu_name_contains` `RTX PRO 4500`) and fetched the package.

### What happened

```
AWS_CANDIDATE_FAILED   (12:59:07 -> 13:10:30, ~11.4 minutes wall, ~9-10 minutes of instance time)

_inner_runner.py, line 12:
  require(MANIFEST['inputs_sha256'] == CONFIG['inputs_sha256'], 'MANIFEST_INPUT_INDEX_HASH')
ValueError: TYPED_BOOTSTRAP:MANIFEST_INPUT_INDEX_HASH
```

**The failure is at the container's FIRST check — before the encoder is built, before phase A, before any
training.** The fail-closed bootstrap refused, which is what it is for. `compute_cleanup: COMPLETE`; the instance is
gone. Nothing was trained.

### What it cost, in two different senses

| | |
|---|---|
| **cash spent** | **≈ $0.40** — ~9-10 min of `g7.2xlarge` at `2.52`/h, essentially all of it boot, package install and the refusal |
| **cap committed** | **$15.00** — the reservation, which has **no refund path** (`budget.py` exposes no release/settle) |
| canonical ledger after | cap `5000.00`, reserved **`561.00`** (was `546.00`), unreserved **`4439.00`**, revision 88, 90 entries |
| the reservation row | `cas-meta2-tpntr-20260919193339-111380d9-m-20260917`, `15.00`, `13:01:10` |

Both numbers matter and they are not the same thing: the reservation is a cap commitment, the forty cents is money
that actually left.

**Note the ledger trap again:** the CNCM-tree snapshot still reads revision 84 / reserved `501.00` / 86 entries and
shows **zero** pointer reservations, because the launch wrote to the canonical `meta1` ledger. Reading the wrong
copy would have reported "nothing was spent".

### Diagnosis — most likely mine, and precisely

The smoke admission was frozen at HEAD **`111380d9`**. I then committed the launcher's `--reduced-schedule` fix as
**`65e37a63`** — and **`typed_pointer_launch.py` is itself a member of `PACKAGING_SOURCES`.** M3's fix made
`frozen_inputs()` read every inventory row *out of the commit* so that "committed" and "admitted" are the same
bytes; the config's `inputs_sha256` and the package manifest's now sit on **different sides of my intervening
commit**.

**The gap this exposes, stated plainly:** the launcher verifies packaged sources against the commit at **build**
time, and the worker verifies manifest-vs-config at **run** time, but **nothing re-checks that a frozen admission's
recorded hashes still describe the tree at LAUNCH time.** A freeze is not invalidated when a packaged source
changes afterwards. That is a real missing guard, and it cost ~$0.40 plus a $15 commitment to find instead of
being caught for $0.

### What works, proven on real infrastructure

The reduced-schedule path is **proven end to end up to the bootstrap**: `--reduced-schedule` was accepted, the
smoke-only evidence contract was selected across the process boundary, the reservation was taken, an instance was
created, the package was fetched, and the container started. The earlier refusal
(`POINTER_AUTHORITY_BINDING:evidence_contract_sha256`) is genuinely fixed.

### The next three steps, in order

1. **Re-freeze the smoke admission at the current HEAD**, so the package, the config and the manifest all describe
   the *same* revision. `prepare_smoke.py` writes create-only, so the existing `admission_smoke_001/` must be moved
   aside first — it is committed, so nothing is lost.
2. **Add the missing launch-time guard**: `main()` must refuse when any packaged source's working-tree bytes differ
   from the commit the authority names, so this class of error is caught for **$0**.
3. **Re-launch.** Then `phase_timings.json` gives the measured seconds per update and per evaluation, which sets
   `PASSES` — the lever that decides whether these neurons get enough training to converge.

**The neurons have still never been trained.** The refusal happened before the encoder was built, so this run
moved us no closer to the learning answer. What it bought is a real defect, found on real infrastructure, for about
the price of the two adversarial reviews.

## 2026-09-19 UTC (pre-flight, $0) - CORRECTION: R1-1 IS fixed; my acceptance test went stale and I reported a fixed blocker as open

**I was wrong, and this row exists to correct the record.**

For roughly ten goal rounds I reported R1's BLOCKER 1 — "the label's key test cannot fail" — as **still open**,
based on a mutation that swapped `population_ids` for `eligible_all` at the call site inside `evaluate()`. That
mutation kept printing `SELFTEST_PASS`. **It kept passing because it had become a no-op.**

When the gate reader agent refactored the call path, `population_ids` stopped appearing at the call site at all.
My substitution therefore rewrote a string that no longer existed, changed nothing, and "passed" — which I read as
the fix failing. **I was testing my own stale test, not their fix.** A reader who checks the mechanism
(`ast`-parsed the file for calls into the label path) found it immediately: `self_test()` now *does* call
`evaluate()`, and `evaluate()` calls a factored `primary_keys(...)`.

**The fix is real, and it is stronger than a test.** `evaluate_gates.py:341-366`:

```python
def primary_keys(contract, population, eligible, kind_of):
    if population is None:
        return []
    tier = contract.DESIGN.get('primary_label_tier')
    members = set(contract.TIERS.get(tier, ()))
    keys = sorted(question for question in population['question_ids'] if kind_of.get(question) in members)
    if set(keys) != set(population['question_ids']) or not set(keys) <= set(eligible):
        return []
    return keys
```

Every path out of it is fail-closed. No population → `[]`. A key set that is not **exactly** the population →
`[]`. A key set outside the eligible boundary → `[]`. And the tier is read from the **contract**, so a contract
registering a different tier yields an **empty** set rather than a wider one — which is precisely the FP1
regression (label keyed on the all-kinds result or on DIRECT_READ) made **structurally impossible** rather than
merely detected. `evaluate()` can no longer hand `label_decision` any other question set, whatever anyone edits.

**What went wrong in my process, recorded because the process is the repeatable part.** I wrote a byte-mutation
test and then treated its verdict as authoritative across a refactor of the very code it mutated. A mutation test
is only valid against the exact shape it was written for; when the shape changes it does not fail loudly, it
**silently stops testing**. The correct habit, now adopted: before trusting a mutation result, confirm the mutated
string still exists at the mutated site, and assert the mutation actually changed the file. I did the second half
of that check at the start — `assert tier_line in pinned` — and did not do it at the call site, which is exactly
where it mattered.

**The corrected standing of the two reviews:**

| | |
|---|---|
| R2 | **all findings closed** — B1, B2, B3, M1, M2, M3, M4 |
| R1 | **all findings closed** — R1-1, R1-2, R1-3, R1-4, R1-6, R1-7, R1-8, R1-9, R1-10, R1-11, R1-15 |

Nothing is left open from either adversarial review. **No gate was weakened to reach this state**: population 18,
`MIN_PASSING_B` 7, `REMOVALS` 11, and the frozen population digest are all unchanged, and the reader's fixes were
additive or fail-closed throughout.

## 2026-09-19 UTC (pre-flight, $0) - R1 returns NO-GO as well: the label's key test cannot fail, and Phase A's ordering evidence is a hardcoded literal

**No spend. No AWS call. No study run.** Review R1 (leaks, shortcuts and custody) reported. Verdict **NO-GO**:
2 BLOCKER, 5 MAJOR, 5 MINOR, 3 NOTE, 1 CONFLICT-for-the-owner, **7 REFUTED**. Both reviews are now NO-GO, which
is the pair working as designed — the practice study's second review found what the first redesign created.

### The two blockers

**R1-1 — the build's most important test cannot fail on the regression it names.** The brief called the
flag-copy-only rule "the single most important behavioural test in the build". R1 ran a **15-mutation battery**
against `evaluate_gates.py --self-test` and measured that **M2** (`evaluate()` feeding `label_decision` the
ALL-KINDS result instead of the RELATIONAL population — the exact FP1 regression) and **M3** (keying the label on
the DIRECT_READ tier) both leave the suite **green, exit 0, `SELFTEST_PASS 23 checks`**. The cause is mechanical:
`self_test()` **never calls `evaluate()`**. It supplies the very step it appears to be testing. Also uncaught: the
≥2-kinds rule, the ≥2-worlds rule, the 5-question shortcut subtraction, the reduced-schedule refusal, and the
ceilings note. And **P12 does not exist**: `pytest --collect-only` collects **20** tests, covering only P1, P2, P3,
P9, P10, P11 — while protocol §11.2 calls that file "the test map of §11.3", which requires P1–P14. The practice
study's own file carries the equivalents, so this is unported coverage, not a convention that never existed.

**R1-2 — Phase A's custody ordering is evidence the job asserts but never measures.**
`cncm/typed_pointer_worker.py:857` is `"phase_a_finished_before_the_model_was_seeded": True` **unconditionally**,
with the real status beside it at `:858` inside a report-only optional-fields tuple, so no reader branch can reach
a failure code for it. Phase A is `except Exception → status="FAILED"` and `run()` proceeds; `_seed_and_double_build`
falls back to the sidecar. So **a job whose checkpoint check FAILED still delivers `COMPLETE_TYPED_POINTER_ARM`
and can print `LEARNS_NEW_WORLDS` while its own `model_identity.json` asserts the ordering held** — a false claim
the machine writes about itself. Worse, the restore is what advances all three RNG streams, so the run would have
a different RNG history from the one the registered design assumes. And
`identical_start_with_and_without_the_checkpoint_check` is **definitionally the same number** as
`core_identity_equals_the_practice_start`, so two of the four DG4 booleans are one measurement, not two.

### The finding that matters most to the PRIMARY goal

**R1-23, recorded as a `CONFLICT` for the owner and deliberately NOT acted on.** R1 measured that the registered
**zero-learning** oracle one-hop rules answer **exactly 7 of the frozen 18** questions (`parts` 2 of 2,
`edge_account` 5 of 5), spread over **2 kinds and 5 worlds** — and `MIN_PASSING_B` is **7**, the ≥2-kinds rule
needs 2 and the ≥2-worlds rule needs 2. So every conjunct of `LEARNS_NEW_WORLDS` is satisfiable at `c = 0`
(`p = 1/128 = 0.0078125`) by a rule that learns nothing. The rank-only rule separately covers **5 of 18**
(2 kinds, 2 worlds); the union of the two registered baseline sets is **11 of 18**.

**Not acted on, and why that is correct:** protocol §4.4 and `REVIEW_SYNTHESIS.md` §10.1.1 argue the opposite case
explicitly and the owner confirmed it — applying the checker's own rule to the input *is* the skill under test, so
the one-hop rule is the ceiling to beat, not a reason to delete questions. The gate is the owner's and is frozen;
`MIN_PASSING_B` stays 7 and the population stays 18. **What R1-4 does require is that the number be printed**:
the reader must show `oracle_solvable_in_the_label_population` and `min_passing_b` beside every label, because
today a reader comparing "solves 5 of eligible 5" against "b must be 7" has no way to see that the two are the
same questions. Reporting only; no threshold moves.

### Also fixed or dispatched
`R1-3` the reader crashes with a bare traceback and empty stdout on a malformed study file instead of printing a
VOID reading. `R1-4` the decisive number is not printed (above). `R1-6` the contract says the rank-solvable
questions "are removed instead" — **measured: 5 of 5 remain in the 18**. `R1-7` `required_artifacts` is the run's
own recorded set, safe for the label but not as a completeness claim. `R1-11` and `R1-15` two stale/false
documents. All dispatched to the agents that own those files, with one owner per file.

### Seven REFUTED, recorded because the negatives are load-bearing
R1 suspected and then cleared: the evidence publisher **is** create-only end to end (`.partial` + `os.replace`,
pass logs staged outside `results/`, monotonic progress files, fresh-directory requirement); the reader emits
**no** answer-bearing flag and **no** rank key (node dict is exactly four keys, all eight kinds); `correspondence`
**is** reported with all five ids and a per-kind result; **no** non-claim-bearing run can print a label and all
**ten** reduced keys are covered; the ceilings **do** subtract nothing; **no** baseline is fitted on held answers;
and **the protocol's own §4.3 per-world `CONFLICT:` is FALSE** — both sources agree (98c6352d 6, 28f61f2d 5,
6e1fcbda 6, 22704554 5, 4090ec49 7); it arose from reading a world-id-sorted dict as if it were in listed order.

**The frozen population re-derived clean:** total **18**, by kind `{parts 2, edge_account 5, reach 6, analogy 5,
correspondence 0}`, digest `4b1e3c1f0b8f8be339e281410dc830fac1834432e8d816d67dce58906e90036d` — reproduces exactly.
LK11 also reproduced: **15 of 45** eligible single-record questions, **4 of 18** in the population.

**One process lesson R1 recorded and this log accepts:** it was told to review `00128c20` and HEAD moved three
times underneath it. Its non-learner findings hold at both revisions because `git diff --name-only 00128c20 HEAD`
touches only `CHANGELOG.md`, the smoke plan and `pointer_learner.py` — but a review of a moving tree is worth less
than a review of a tag. The re-review will be run against a **tagged, clean revision**.

## 2026-09-19 UTC (pre-flight, $0) - R2 adversarial review returns NO-GO: three blockers, four majors, one withdrawn finding

**No spend. No AWS call. No study run.** Review R2 (run-killers and pipeline fit) reported against HEAD
`00128c20`. Verdict **NO-GO**. Its findings are the reason the reviews exist: two of the three blockers would each
have burned money before failing, and the third silently destroys an evidence family in **every** job.

| # | severity | finding |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | **BLOCKER** | `CNCM_POINTER_REDUCED_SCHEDULE` is read by the worker and **set by nothing**, so the reduced smoke is impossible — it would run the FULL 1,470-update schedule against a ~$1 approval |
| B2 | **BLOCKER** | the launcher's evidence contract demands all **102** registered names, so a perfect reduced run returns `completed_deliverable: false`, `main()` exits 1, and `execute_study.py:65` stops the study |
| B3 | **BLOCKER** | `ABLATION_COMPONENTS` misses 2 of the 46 container state-dict keys, so `POINTER_ABLATION_COVERAGE` fails and **all 10 ablation artifacts are dropped from every job** while the envelope still reports `COMPLETE_TYPED_POINTER_ARM` |
| M1 | MAJOR | the cost projection omits the 598.2 s of non-worker overhead and phase B, and compares against `projected_worker_seconds` rather than instance time — so `within_owner_threshold: True` can be written for a job over the owner's 11,000 s line, and that projection is what decides `PASSES` |
| M2 | MAJOR | a report-only ablation failure is delivered as `COMPLETE` with no machine-readable trace, so "the ablations ran" and "the ablations never started" are indistinguishable |
| M3 | MAJOR | `frozen_inputs_hash()` hashes the **working tree** while the package is built from `git show` |
| M4 | MAJOR | `STRATIFIED_BATCHES_DIGEST` is pinned to the **10-pass** schedule, so setting `PASSES` — the study's one open value and the whole point of the smoke — turns the test suite red and makes `holdout_audit.py` exit 1 with a misleading `SOURCE_SCHEDULE_IS_NOT_THE_BASE_SCHEDULE` |

**Measured by R2, not argued.** B1: rendered bootstrap 2,549 B, `os.environ` occurs **once** (for
`CAS_META2_RUN_TEACHING_CYCLE`), `CNCM_POINTER_REDUCED_SCHEDULE` **zero** times, and the launcher source does not
contain the name. B2: a reduced run produces **40** names against a contract requiring **102** — **59** registered
names it would never write. B3: 44 of 46 keys covered; the `round_mix` prefix tuple carries **six names that match
nothing**, an abandoned design left behind when three mix scalars became one 3-element `Parameter`. M4: the same
literal is asserted at `tests/test_typed_pointer_study.py:312-313` and `holdout_audit.py:136`.

**One finding withdrawn by the reviewer itself, and recorded because withdrawing it matters.** R2's first pass
printed that `updates` escaped the `claim_bearing` guard; on re-measurement the exhaustive sweep returned
`KEYS WHERE claim_bearing STAYS TRUE WHILE THE PLAN CHANGES: []`, so **all ten reduced keys are covered** and the
finding is REFUTED. That is the correct behaviour from an adversarial reviewer, and it is the same conclusion an
independent orchestrator check reached earlier from `typed_pointer_worker.py:295-298`.

**R2 also confirmed nine of its twelve checklist items PASS**, several of which we had only asserted: the phase
order (phase A at `:1536-1540` before the seed and double build at `:1542-1545`, phase B last at `:1573-1576`);
counters equal as 11-tuples; the schema handshake identical in both directions with `TYPED_POINTER_FAILED` in
`failure_statuses`; deterministic mode clean for every op the learner and the `^6` block use, with only a
deliberately self-overlapping `copy_` control raising; **zero `assert` in all eight spend-path files**; all LF
**checked by bytes**; and all five owner labels byte-verbatim including the em dash, with
`owner_decisions_in_contract_text() == [True] * 5`.

**Fixes dispatched in parallel, one owner per file so no two agents collide:** B1 + B2 + M3 to the launch-path
agent; B3 + M1 + M2 to the worker agent; M4 to the contract/test/audit agent. The learner's one-line fail-closed
shape guard landed separately as `5fc1f57b`, verified green (**20 passed in 140.58s**) before commit.

**One residual risk R2 could not settle, carried forward honestly:** whether `index_select` backward raises under
`torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)` **on the CUDA device**. It is clean on CPU. If it raises, it does so at
the first graph forward inside `stage_batch` — after preflight, after phase A and after the double build. Only the
instance can answer it, and the worker has no instrumentation that would catch it earlier.

## 2026-09-19 UTC (pre-flight, $0) - Two governance defects and a stale contract flag fixed; smoke cost model corrected; four drivers built

**No spend. No AWS call. No study run.** Found while finishing the build. Every one of these was found by a builder or
by an independent seam check, not by the design reviews.

### Governance: the owner's own words were wrong in the contract

`pointer_contract.DESIGN["owner_approval"]` is copied by `prepare_study.py` into **all three** signed per-job
`owner.json` records, and protocol §0.2 says *"The selected labels are recorded verbatim."* It carried **four** of the
five decisions and had damaged the third.

1. **The em dash was gone.** `"Yes — require the relational kinds."` was stored as `"Yes -- require the relational
   kinds."` **Root cause:** `pointer_contract.py` is **pure ASCII** (verified: 0 characters above U+007F), so an
   ASCII-only build step transliterated the em dash to `--`. Fixed with the ASCII source escape `\u2014`, which
   evaluates to the real em dash while keeping the file pure ASCII. The value is now byte-for-byte the protocol's.
2. **The fifth decision was missing entirely.** `"Run it as the transfer test, label honestly (Recommended)"` —
   protocol §0.2 item 4, `docs/CNCM_TYPED_POINTER_STUDY_v0.1.md:64` — was absent from the contract. Added.

All five labels are now present verbatim, checked against `docs/CNCM_TYPED_POINTER_STUDY_v0.1.md:52` (1), `:57` (2),
`:61` (3), `:64` (4), `:69` (5). Verified by import: five of five present, the flattened form absent. `prepare_study.py`
independently reports the em dash as U+2014 in the owner records it writes.

### A stale flag that claimed the gate was still open

`pointer_contract.py` carried `"gate_population_open": True` from the design phase. It contradicted the rest of its own
file: `REMOVALS` is filled with the frozen 11 removals across four classes and `relational_population` returns
`population_complete: True` against `VERIFIED_POPULATION` (18) and `VERIFIED_POPULATION_DIGEST`
`4b1e3c1f0b8f8be339e281410dc830fac1834432e8d816d67dce58906e90036d`. Nothing read it, so it manufactured a false
statement about the study instead of causing a failure. Now `False`. `verify_contract.py` still reports
**ALL PASS (32 checks, 0 failed)**.

### The smoke could not have run at all, and cost more than budgeted

Two defects in `pre_design/AWS_SMOKE_S10_PLAN.md`, both before any spend.

1. **A reload-enabled run needs TWO course evaluations, not one.** `cncm/typed_pointer_worker.py:190` —
   `require(0 in evaluations and passes in evaluations, "POINTER_PLAN_RELOAD_EVALUATIONS")`. The plan budgeted a
   single course evaluation, so its shrunken schedule with reload and replay would have been **refused outright**.
2. **Corrected cost: worker clock ≈ 771 s, not 470 s; ≈ $0.96 of instance time, not $0.75.** The first course pass in
   any job is also the cold one — measured `300.51911266499997` s cold against `173.78626021600007` s warm in the
   practice receipts (`course_p00` vs `course_p01`) — so the two course rows are 300 s and 174 s, not 174 s once.
   `598.2` s of non-worker overhead + 771 s ⇒ 1,369 s ≈ **$0.96 at $2.52/hour**, which is 96 % of the approved $1 with
   no margin, and the S1–S9 checks that ride in the same job cross it.

**This is now an owner decision and no launch happens until it is answered.** The plan's own kill rule — kill rather
than exceed the approval if the measurement shows the estimate was low by more than ~25 % — would have fired mid-run,
because the one-evaluation estimate was low by **64 %**. The plan asks for a **$1.50** cap. Nothing has been spent.

### The four drivers are built

`records/typed_pointer_001/`: `prepare_study.py` 9,675 B, `execute_study.py` 4,627 B, `preflight_dry_run.py` 47,491 B,
`holdout_audit.py` 9,501 B — all LF (0 carriage returns), all compiling. Verified at $0, no AWS and no network call:
`holdout_audit.py` prints `HOLDOUT_AUDIT_PASS` re-deriving held `668e6ce7…ab1d`, excluded `b82108e1…2621` and source
schedule `1772c0a5…d275`; `prepare_study.py`'s record shapes pass the **launcher's own** `validate_request`,
`validate_authority` and `validate_owner` on real job names and a 63-character bucket; and the dry run delivered seed
`20260917` clean at $0 — status `COMPLETE_TYPED_POINTER_ARM`, 1470 + 16 + 2 = 1488 updates, the 11 `MAIN_COUNTERS`
keys matching `expected_main_delta`, 99/99 artifacts.

## 2026-09-19 UTC (pre-flight, $0) - Two defects found and fixed in the AWS smoke plan, and five build seams verified by import

**No spend. No AWS call. No study run.** Work done while the last four build files (`prepare_study.py`,
`execute_study.py`, `preflight_dry_run.py`, `holdout_audit.py`) and the three reconciling files finished.

**Two real defects, both in `records/typed_pointer_001/pre_design/AWS_SMOKE_S10_PLAN.md`, both corrected before any
money was spent.** Both would have cost a paid run or produced a WRONG pass count.

1. **The smoke's own PASS rule could not be satisfied by the schedule it prescribes.** §5 read *"every name in
   `EXPECTED_ARTIFACTS` is present"*, but `EXPECTED_ARTIFACTS` is derived from the **registered** plan
   (`cncm/typed_pointer_worker.py:237`, `expected_artifact_names(FULL_PLAN)`) while §3 runs a **shrunken** one, which
   writes the smaller set for the plan it actually ran. As written, the smoke had to be marked FAIL for doing exactly
   what §3 says. Corrected to the worker's own recorded `required_artifacts` — the set it writes into
   `schedule_plan.json` and enforces at `cncm/typed_pointer_worker.py:1411-1414`. **Not a relaxation:** on a full run
   `required_artifacts` *is* `EXPECTED_ARTIFACTS`, and `schedule_plan.json` records both sets plus
   `artifacts_differ_from_the_registered_contract`, so the reading is checkable. The corrected rule *is* the worker's
   own check, quoted rather than paraphrased.
2. **The measurement list omitted `MAX_CORE_CALLS`, which the contract explicitly requires it to re-check.** `§4`
   listed seconds-per-update, the deadline projection, the end-to-end path and the phase-A ordering — but
   `pointer_contract.py:107-110` says the smoke *"must re-check `MAX_CORE_CALLS` … the core-call budget may bind
   before the update budget does. Both are recorded in DESIGN and must be reconciled before the study is frozen."*
   The pointer machine calls the registered `^6` block once per node per round (~4 nodes × 8 rounds = 32 per world
   read) against the old machine's 8. Added as §4 item 3: read `core_forward_calls` per pass from the smoke's own
   counters, divide `MAX_CORE_CALLS = 600000` by the measured per-pass calls, and set
   **`PASSES = min(deadline-limited, core-call-limited)`**. Setting `PASSES` from the clock alone risked a job that
   trains happily and then dies on the core-call limit **after the spend**.

**Also corrected: the projection formula was hard-coded to one pass count.** §4.1 read
`598.2 + 1470 × s_per_update + …`. The stratified schedule runs **147 updates per pass** at **1176 presentations
per pass** against the practice study's 818 (+358, +43.8 %), and 1470 is `10 × 147` — right only at `PASSES = 10`
and wrong for every other value. Now written as `PASSES × 147 × s_per_update`, with a note that measured
per-update cost must be read against the stratified figure (the practice study's ~3.0 s/update was at 5.56
presentations per update; the stratified pass runs 8, so ~1.438 × is expected).

**Five build seams verified independently by import, not by reading prose.** `$0`, `numpy` only, no torch.

| seam | verified value |
|---|---|
| worker artifact contract | `EXPECTED_ARTIFACTS` = **99** names, all unique, `EVIDENCE_REQUIRED_COUNT` = **102** = 99 + 3 ✓ |
| artifacts scale with `PASSES` | 99 = `2 × 10 + 79`, i.e. `2 × PASSES + 79`; recomputes at freeze, so a changed `PASSES` moves the contract with it rather than breaking it |
| counter key set | learner `COUNTER_KEYS` and contract `MAIN_COUNTERS` are the **same 11 keys in the same order**; `slot_forwards` absent from both ✓ |
| rank feature | `DESIGN["node_rank_feature"]` is `False`; `NODE_FEATURE_COUNT` is redefined from it at `pointer_learner.py:352` ✓ |
| status vocabulary | `SUCCESS_STATUS` / `FAILED_STATUS` / `DELIVERED_STATUS` match the practice study's three-way shape exactly, and the launcher's `failure_statuses` lists `TYPED_POINTER_FAILED` ✓ |

**The smoke cannot be promoted to a label even by mistake** — verified, not assumed. A reduced schedule sets
`claim_bearing = False` (`cncm/typed_pointer_worker.py:273-277`) and records `mode: "REDUCED"`, and the frozen gate
program refuses such a receipt outright: `records/typed_pointer_001/evaluate_gates.py:130` carries the void path
`I1:<seed>:REDUCED_SCHEDULE:<mode>` — *"the job did not run the REGISTERED plan, so it is not a claim-bearing run
and may not print a label."*

**Budget constants re-read, not recalled:** `MAX_CORE_CALLS = 600000`, `WORKER_DEADLINE_SECONDS = 12300`,
`MAX_WALL_SECONDS = 12600`, `INSTANCE_SECONDS_MAX = 14400`, `RESERVATION_USD = 15.00`,
`LEARNER_MAX_UPDATES_CEILING = 3544`, `MAX_PASSES_PERMITTED = 24`, `INTERFERENCE_UPDATES = 16`,
`PASSES = 10` (**still the one open value, inherited from the practice study and marked NOT FREEZABLE AS-IS**).

## 2026-09-19 UTC (later again) - Reviews re-run; the gate's power measured; the pointer contract built and verified

$0.00. No AWS call, no launch. Owner instruction this session: **"REMEMBER OUR MAIN GOAL IS GETTING THOSE NEURONS TO
LEARN"**, which reframed the priority from measurement to learning.

THE GATE CANNOT DETECT A REAL IMPROVEMENT (pure arithmetic, `pre_design/GATE_FEASIBILITY.md`, `gate_power.py`). The
three-seed requirement does the damage. At 17 honest relational questions a genuine 40 % improvement clears one seed
with probability 0.5522 and all three with 0.1683; at 11 questions even 50 % gives 0.0207. Regressions are brutal: the
smallest passing count is b=7 with none, 10 with one, 12 with two, 14 with three. Every caveat makes the real power
lower, so the table is an upper bound. Findings FP1-FP3 guard well against a false positive; this is the opposite
risk, a gate so tight it returns `NO_TRANSFER` whether or not the machine learned — the project's own word for that is
VACUOUS. Not acted on; the gate is unchanged; three options are recorded for the owner.

LEARNING LEVERS (`pre_design/LEARNING_LEVERS.md`). (1) The machine was stopped while still learning: mean loss fell
monotonically 1.2879780873 → 0.3981766156 over ten passes and used 5,465.811116 s of a 12,300 s deadline, 44.4 %.
(2) A registered ceiling caps training at 13 passes: `LEARNER_MAX_UPDATES_CEILING` 2048 with
`main_updates + 16 <= 2048`, and `pointer_contract.py:66` inherits it; 14 passes is refused. (3) **Held-back RECORD
accuracy rose 5→23, 7→21, 7→15 of 127 while the held-back QUESTION gate moved +3, 0, −1 of 45** — the neurons were
learning about unpractised worlds even through the broken input, and the gate's unit could not see it, because a
question counts only when all of its records are right. Recommendation: raise the ceiling and train to convergence,
and report held-back accuracy per record as a second reported number that is never a label.

THE LEAKS LENS found the input hands over two relational kinds: the proposed input's own `contains` adjacency solves
`parts` 5/5 and `edge_account` 5/5 with no learned weights, wider than the known FP2 rule (3/5). R10, a rank-only rule,
solves 4/9 held `reach`; re-derived independently here and confirmed exactly, which also exposed a **constant `VIA n1`
answer solving 4/9 on the complementary set** — two policies together solve 8/9. Role_set is solved 6/6 by a public
clause-prefix map and 6/6 by a position-free token bag. **No cheap rule solves any held `correspondence` or `analogy`
question**, their paired worlds share no node label, and 54 + 36 trained records back them — so those are the clean,
hard kinds where a genuine transferable skill would show.

THE CONTRACT IS BUILT AND VERIFIED. `pointer_contract.py` (32,066 bytes, LF, ASCII-only) and
`tests/test_typed_pointer_study.py` (17,560 bytes). Tests: **11 passed in 76.45s** (run by me), practice baseline
**24 passed** untouched. An independent verifier (`pre_design/verify_contract.py`) passes **18 of 18**: the 18
checkpoint MATERIAL rows equal `checkpoint_pins.json`, total 15,259,777 bytes, the `tpntr` bucket is exactly 63
characters, `MIN_PASSING_B` is 7, the hold-out digests equal the practice study's own, and an unfilled removals
placeholder reports `population_complete: False` rather than looking finished. Nothing existing was edited.

CORRECTIONS OF MY OWN ERRORS, all found by review: two wrong `model_state_identity` digests in
`INTERFACE_SPEC_SHARED.md` (the real seeds 20260918/19 values are `35081019…` and `91279389…`, agreed by two sources
in each receipt); the bucket length for `tpntr` stated as 62 when it is 63; and a verification test of mine that
asserted the wrong guard intent, corrected against the builder's implementation.

## 2026-09-17 UTC - The rectangular solve is built and tested, and what it measures closes a route

$0.00. Pure Python, project's pure modules only. No torch, no device, no model, no training, no new
run. Code: `cncm/analytic_decompose.decompose_from_free_readout_rectangular` (new). Tests:
`tests/test_analytic_decompose_rectangular.py` (13 new, 189 passing with the existing module tests).
Record: `records/encoder_capacity_001/RECTANGULAR_REALISATION_RESULT.md`.

THE STEP THAT WAS OWED. decompose_from_free_readout builds ^6 parameters with T = R^-1 Wt so the
frozen readout R behaves as a chosen free readout W. It REQUIRES nclass == core_width, and the
registered readout is [90, 64] - so it REFUSES the real readout, and every analytic measurement since
round 22 has had to work on a 64-row submatrix. The new function takes nclass > core_width via

    T = (RtR)^-1 Rt Wt        minimum-norm least squares

and it CANNOT realise every W: R T always lands in the column space of R, dimension at most
core_width. So it realises the ORTHOGONAL PROJECTION of Wt onto that space and REPORTS THE RESIDUAL.
13 tests defend it, including registered shapes, forward(x) = T tanh(x), five refusal paths, numerical
agreement with the square construction when nclass == D, exactness when Wt = R B, and - the test that
matters - that in the unreachable case R T lies in the column space while Wt - R T is ORTHOGONAL TO
EVERY COLUMN OF R, with the reported residual equal to the largest entry of that leftover.

THE MEASUREMENT IS A NEGATIVE, AND IT IS THE NUMBER THAT WAS MISSING. Round 30 fitted a free readout
on tanh(encoded) and scored the many-decision half at 5.0167% -> 12.0401% with definition at
48.5714% - a SUBSTITUTED readout, not the machine's. Whether it could be installed was open. Now
measured, five folds by question, 299 questions, real [90, 64] readout:

    fold 0: residual 0.1475689756   exact False
    fold 1: residual 0.1440038168   exact False
    fold 2: residual 0.1442578014   exact False
    fold 3: residual 0.1850843724   exact False
    fold 4: residual 0.1716314452   exact False

    kind             q   recorded   direct W   through T   T - direct
    edge_account    70     0.0000%    1.4286%     0.0000%    -1.4286%
    role_set        36     8.3333%    8.3333%    11.1111%    +2.7778%
    parts           53    22.6415%    3.7736%     3.7736%    +0.0000%
    context         70     0.0000%    4.2857%     0.0000%    -4.2857%
    definition      70     0.0000%   48.5714%     8.5714%   -40.0000%
    MANY           299     5.0167%   14.3813%     4.0134%   -10.3679%

THE REGISTERED PATH LANDS AT 4.0134% - BELOW THE RUN'S OWN 5.0167%. The residual is never exactly
zero in any fold. ROUND 30's FITTED-READOUT GAIN IS AN IDEALISATION THAT CANNOT BE INSTALLED, and
definition alone loses 40.0000 points. FIX A CANNOT IMPROVE THE MANY-DECISION HALF BY THIS ROUTE AND
NO GPU RUN SHOULD BE SPENT TRYING. The square requirement was never arbitrary: with R square,
R T = Wt is EXACTLY satisfiable, which is why Fix A's 62.94%/62.06% on focus identification is real;

chance: ordered by margin, the truly-absent node lands outside the top three 60.9863% of the time. A
DECLARED CONSTRAINT CONVERTS AN ABSOLUTE-THRESHOLD PROBLEM INTO A RANKING PROBLEM.

FIRST FIX THAT WORKS ON THE TRAINED MACHINE'S OWN SCORES. Round 30's fitted readout was a
substituted, hypothetical readout and cost more than it gained overall. This uses the recorded scores
exactly as the run produced them, and it needs no new information path, no capacity, no weights, no
encoder change. It is structural and free: the constraint is a field of the packet the machine
already receives, and score_decisions never reads it.

IT CORRECTS ROUND 27'S REFUSAL OF ROUND 26. Round 26 found the architecture composes jointly for
exactly one kind and proposed extending the joint branch; round 27 refused, because three of four
binary kinds read their bits at or below chance and a composer cannot compose what was never read.
Round 32 corrects the correction: THERE IS SOMETHING FOR THE COMPOSER TO DO - not a joint likelihood
but a DECLARED CONSTRAINT. The bits were at chance AS THRESHOLDS and are well above chance AS
RANKINGS, and a declared cardinality is what converts one into the other.

THE GENERALISATION IS ARCHITECTURAL AND UNTRIED. Every kind carries declared structure in the packet
that nothing reads: edge_account (the focus world's `contains`, size declared per world), parts
(direct targets of the supplied `whole`), role_set (ALL_AND_ONLY_SOURCE_GROUNDED_FOCAL_ROLES),
definition (FOCUS_WORLD_DECLARATION_IDENTITY - one world's declaration, not three free choices). THE
ARCHITECTURE ALREADY DECLARES, PER KIND, WHAT A LEGAL ANSWER IS. NOTHING READS IT.

and read 0.0000% for every arm in all six runs - the same signature that exposed the edge-direction
bug. context has TWO fields, so with setting_ref wrong no participant decoding can be correct and the
arm measured nothing. Fixed by holding setting_ref at the truth; both arms are printed in the
artifact so the confound is visible rather than smoothed away.

$0.00. Pure Python, standard library only. No torch, no device, no model, no training, no new run.
Record `records/encoder_capacity_001/EDGE_DIRECTION_CORRECTION.md`; three result records carry
correction pointers; `MACHINE_STATE_001/STATUS.md` extended with Limit 7 and its `edge_account` rows
corrected.

THE BUG, FOUND BY AN IMPOSSIBLE READING. Round 31's field decomposition gave each kind its one true
field and re-assembled. role_set and parts reached 100.0000%. edge_account reported 0.0000% - and
edge_account has exactly ONE field, so that arm hands the answer over outright. A kind that scores
0.0000% WHEN GIVEN THE ANSWER is unreachable, not hard. probe_edge_reachability.py then printed, for
a world with 4 declared edges:

    true PRESENT slots: 8 of 16   (truth has 4 edges)
    only in assembled: [(n1,n0), (n2,n1), (n2,n3), (n3,n0)]     only in truth: []

CAUSE: `tuple(sorted((row["source"], row["target"])))` in the per-slot truth. checker._edges is
frozenset((source, target)) - an ORDERED pair - and assemble_answer emits slot["coordinates"] as
[source, target]. Sorting collapsed the two directions onto one key, marking (a,b) AND (b,a) PRESENT
and exactly doubling the edge count. Fixed to the directed tuple in measure_bits_read.py,
measure_field_bottleneck.py, measure_binding_ceiling.py, measure_fitted_readout_score.py and
measure_binding_variants.py, all patched in place. Re-run of the same probe: "true PRESENT slots: 4
of 16", "assembled == admissible_answers[0] ? True", on two questions. SCOPE: edge_account only -
the other three binary kinds key on a SINGLE coordinate, where sorting cannot conflate anything.

WHY THE 229/229 VALIDATION DID NOT CATCH IT, AND COULD NOT. That check compared the machine's
SUBMITTED ANSWER to the answer the project's checker derives, and passed six-for-six, twice. It
validated the TRUTH DERIVATION, correctly. It never touched true_symbol, the mapping built on top of
it. A VALIDATION THAT CONFIRMS YOUR ORACLE DOES NOT VALIDATE THE LAYER YOU BUILD ON IT. What caught
it was not a validation but an impossible result; contradictions are cheaper detectors than
validations. Both lessons are now standing practice.

CORRECTED NUMBERS, edge_account per bit, six runs:

                       as published (rounds 27-31)   corrected
    per-bit accuracy   52.3810% - 53.5471%          62.7794% - 67.3469%
    truth PRESENT      40.4276%                     20.2138%
    chance             51.8326%                     67.7444%
    best constant      59.5724%                     79.7862%
    vs best constant   -6.0253 to -7.1914           -12.4393 to -17.0068
    exact answers      0.0000%, all six             0.0000%, all six (unchanged)

THE MACHINE LOOKS BETTER IN ABSOLUTE TERMS AND WORSE AGAINST ITS BASELINES: below chance in FOUR of

## 2026-09-17 UTC - A fitted readout lifts half the exam and costs more than it gains, and the constraint is that there is only ONE map

$0.00. Pure Python, standard library only. No torch, no device, no learned run weights, no forward
pass of the block, no training, no new run. Record
`records/encoder_capacity_001/FITTED_READOUT_RESULT.md`; `MACHINE_STATE_001/STATUS.md` extended with
Limit 6.

THE BRIDGE ROUND 29 LEFT UNBUILT. Round 29 measured that a fitted linear readout reads the
many-decision bits far better than the trained block (context 96.3758% against 40.8240%) and
separately that a memoriser converts into -6.7033 points at the answer level. Those are different
readouts, and the one that matters - the construction `decompose_from_free_readout` installs,
score_c = W[:,c] . tanh(x) with W = (XtX + lam I)^-1 Xt Y - had never been scored. Now it is:
features tanh(normalize(packet + slot)) rebuilt exactly from the checksum-gated frozen bytes, and the
score table handed to the project's OWN `slots.score_decisions` and `slots.assemble_answer`. Only the
readout is substituted.

THE HARNESS VALIDATED ITSELF. Its `frozen (recorded)` row is not a fit - it re-scores the run's own
submitted answers - and it reproduces the run exactly: MANY DECISIONS 5.0167% against the recorded
5.02%, ALL 33.5106% to four decimals, admissible-set sizes {1: 461, 2: 15, 6: 36, 9: 52} summing to
564. An earlier draft compared answers to `admissible_answers[0]` only and reported analogy at 0.00%
and correspondence at 59.0909%; caught and fixed to admissible-SET membership before shipping.

THE RESULT, TWO RUNS.

    family            q    recorded     corr IN the fit   corr EXCLUDED
    ONE DECISION    265     65.6604%          21.5094%            -
    MANY DECISIONS  299      5.0167%          12.0401%       16.0535%
    ALL             564     33.5106%          16.4894%       18.6170%

FIRST CHANGE MEASURED TO LIFT THE MANY-DECISION HALF: +7.0234 points with correspondence sharing the
fit, +11.0368 without. definition goes from 0 of 70 to 18-31 of 70 (0.0000% -> 25.7143%-44.2857%);
edge_account 0.0000% -> 4.2857%; context 0.0000% -> 2.8571%; role_set 8.3333% -> 11.1111%. parts FALLS
7.5472 points. Lambda is not load-bearing: 0.1 through 0.0001 give an identical 15.3846% on MANY, and
tanh(x) and x give identical numbers at every lambda.

AND IT IS A NET LOSS: -17.0213 points overall, because the one-decision half falls 44.1510 points.
Putting correspondence into the same solve costs the many-decision half 4.0 points, because its
64-symbol vocabularies dominate a single 90-class ridge fit.

THE ARCHITECTURAL CONSEQUENCE, WHICH IS THE POINT. T = R^-1 Wt is a SINGLE 64x64 map and the machine
has ONE block and ONE readout. One shared map cannot serve both families: fit it for the
many-decision kinds and the one-decision kinds lose 44.1510 points; fit it for both and it is worse
than the tuned machine on both halves. correspondence's 93.18%-96.59% depends on RELATIVE score
structure that a one-hot ridge fit does not preserve - so the frozen readout plus the trained block is
a better score generator for the joint candidate decoder than a one-hot ridge readout is. That is a
separate and unexpected finding.

AND THE CONTRACT ALREADY ADMITS TWO PLASTICITY GROUPS: ('operation:^6', 'bias_update_head'). The run
uses one. The bias_update_head is a real 4,096-parameter module absent from the run's module (Fix C,
OS-027), and /memory appears as a target_path in the recorded aws_saved_repair_v8_001 lifecycle
receipt. THIS IS THE FIRST EVIDENCE THAT THE SECOND GROUP IS NOT DECORATIVE: one shared map is
measurably the constraint, and the exam splits cleanly into two families needing different score
structure.

ALSO ESTABLISHED: the closer an answer is to a verbatim packet field, the worse the machine does, read
straight off `checker._derive_values`. edge_account, context and definition - whose answers ARE
verbatim packet fields - score 0.0000% each, 210 of 564 questions; parts (one filter step) scores
22.6415%; role_set (derivation) 8.3333%. THE MACHINE HAS NO READ PATH AND THE TASKS THAT REQUIRE ONE
ARE PRECISELY THE TASKS IT FAILS ENTIRELY.

NOT LICENSED: that a run would realise either figure - the readouts are fitted out-of-fold on the same
564 questions, and `decompose_from_free_readout` today REFUSES the real readout because it requires
nclass == core_width while the frozen readout is [90, 64]. The rectangular solve is an engineering
step, untaken. OPEN: why definition responds to a fitted readout (+25.71 to +44.29) and context barely
does (+2.86 to +4.29), when both are verbatim-field kinds of the same size and same slot arity.

## 2026-09-17 UTC - The architecture has exactly ONE composition mechanism, hard-coded for ONE kind, and it is the kind that works

$0.00. Pure standard library. JSON and source reading only. No torch, no device, no target opened.
Record `records/encoder_capacity_001/COMPOSITION_BRANCH_RESULT.md`; `ARGMAX_CHECK_RESULT.md`
corrected; OS-026 restated.

THE MECHANISM, IN FULL. cas_meta2/typed_teaching/slots.py, score_decisions, lines 283-296:
    if plan["kind"] != "correspondence":
        return _decision(plan, {slot["slot_id"]: max(slot["symbols"],
                            key=lambda symbol: scores[slot["slot_id"]][symbol])
                            for slot in plan["slots"]})
    winner = max(_candidate_scores(plan, scores), key=lambda row: row["log_probability"])
    return _decision(plan, {}, winner["candidate_id"])
TWO BRANCHES AND NOTHING ELSE. Every kind except correspondence is decided by a PLAIN INDEPENDENT
PER-SLOT max. correspondence is decided by a JOINT MAP OVER CANDIDATE BIJECTIONS - _candidate_scores
computes, per candidate, log P(NO_MATCH) + log P(MAPPING) + SUM_pairs log P(pair), normalised
hierarchically - and returns selected = {} with the whole decision in correspondence_candidate_id.
THE ARCHITECTURE'S ONLY COMPOSITION MECHANISM IS THREE LINES OF A BRANCH, APPLIED TO EXACTLY ONE OF
THE EIGHT QUESTION KINDS.

THE ATTRIBUTION, NOW EXACT. Round 25 found 422 non-argmax `selected` values per run-file and
attributed them to "forced slots and the joint correspondence candidate". MEASURED BY KIND: every one
of the 422 is a CORRESPONDENCE slot and every one is MISSING FROM `selected`; no other kind
contributes a single one (analogy 0/70, context 0/337, definition 0/210, edge_account 0/1029, parts
0/207, reach 0/107, role_set 0/216). "Forced slots" is WRONG and that clause is withdrawn: a forced
slot has one symbol whose argmax is itself, so it matches. The cause is score_decisions returning
selected = {} for correspondence.

THE SHARPEST STATEMENT OF THE ARCHITECTURAL GAP SO FAR. Beside the measured after-rates:

  edge_account    plain per-slot max                     0.00%
and these hold in ALL SIX RUNS. THE ONE KIND THE CONTRACT COMPOSES JOINTLY IS THE ONE KIND THAT
WORKS. This is not a correlation - it is a three-line branch in the code, and the kinds on the
independent side include all three that have never once been answered in 1,260 attempts.

WHAT THIS DOES TO FIX B. It is no longer an invention; it is THE GENERALISATION OF A MECHANISM THE
CONTRACT ALREADY HAS, ALREADY APPLIES, AND HAS ALREADY PROVEN. The joint branch does exactly what
cncm/composition.py proposes, in miniature: form a joint score over the whole answer rather than a
per-slot one; take one winner for the entity the answer is about; assemble the rest from that winner
rather than deciding each part independently.

AND IT RESTATES THE BOUNDARY QUESTION MORE SHARPLY. OS-026 asked "should composition be added?" -
but composition is already in the contract. The question is now: SHOULD THE JOINT DECISION THAT
ALREADY EXISTS FOR correspondence BE EXTENDED TO THE KINDS WHOSE ANSWERS ARE READINGS OF ONE ENTITY?
That is a question about an existing mechanism rather than a proposed one, and it is still an
authority decision. OS-026 is restated in those terms.

## 2026-09-17 UTC - TENTH SELF-CORRECTION: the "26-point information loss" was my probe's penalty, not the block

$0.00. torch used only to deserialise checkpoints. Script
`records/encoder_capacity_001/check_information_loss.py` (result `information_loss_check.txt`),
record `INFORMATION_LOSS_WITHDRAWN.md`; `STAGE_INFORMATION_RESULT.md` annotated WITHDRAWN.

WHAT WAS CLAIMED. Last round reported that the registered ^6 architecture reduces focus-world
decodability from 63.83% to 37.3%, called it a 26.5-point loss, and concluded the block "discards 42%
of the focus world's linearly available information" - and explained the analytic initialisation as
"bypassing that loss". THAT CONCLUSION DOES NOT FOLLOW FROM THE MEASUREMENT AND IT IS FALSE. The
probe used a FIXED RIDGE PENALTY OF LAMBDA = 1.0 on every feature set, and an invertible linear map
followed by a mildly nonlinear tanh preserves information; what it changes is CONDITIONING.

THE CHECK THAT REFUTES IT - a penalty sweep, same features, same folds:
  features            l=1e-06   l=0.0001    l=0.01     l=0.1       l=1      l=10
  x (encoded slot)     64.54%     64.54%    64.54%    64.72%    63.65%    56.03%
  tanh(x)              64.72%     64.72%    64.72%    64.72%    63.83%    56.03%
  ^6_untrained(x)      65.07%     66.31%    60.99%    51.06%    37.23%    21.81%
  ^6_trained(x)        66.49%     66.31%    61.17%    55.32%    37.41%    19.86%
AT LAMBDA = 1e-4 THE BLOCK READS 66.31% AND 66.31%. At lambda = 1e-6, 65.07% and 66.49% - HIGHER
than the raw encoded slot's 64.72%. The block does NOT discard information; it preserves all of it
and at a suitable penalty slightly more. The 37% was the probe misreading an ill-conditioned space.

WHITENING CONFIRMS IT. Standardising each set to unit variance and classifying at lambda=1: x 66.31%,
tanh(x) 66.13%, ^6_untrained(x) 65.96%, ^6_trained(x) 56.38%. The untrained block's output is
informative and merely badly scaled.

AND THE MAP IS NOT RANK DEFICIENT. Singular values of merge_left@left + merge_right@right:
untrained largest 0.707129, smallest 6.265253e-05, ratio 1.129e4, rank holding 99% of the sum 56 of
64; trained largest 14.851412, smallest 3.353687e-03, ratio 4.428e3, rank 49 of 64. Full rank,
badly conditioned - information is stretched unevenly, not destroyed.

WHAT SURVIVES. The saturation sweep stands: it involved no trained parameter, decodability is flat to
slightly rising from s=1 to s=32 and only degrades past s=64, so scalar saturation is not a
mechanism. And the "training changes nothing" finding is STRENGTHENED: at lambda=1e-4 the untrained
and trained blocks read 66.31% and 66.31%, identical to two decimal places.

WHAT THE TRAINED MACHINE'S 1.24% THEREFORE IS NOT: it is not information loss in the block. The
information is present at 66.3% through the trained block, and it is present for the FROZEN readout
too - REACHABLE_SOLUTION_RESULT.md measured that the frozen rows realise ANY linear classifier
exactly, at residual 2.798e-14. A linear read of ^6_trained(x) at lambda=1e-4 reaches 66.31%; the
trained machine reaches 1.24%. So the failure is entirely in how the frozen readout's argmax is
arranged, which is what CONCENTRATION_SOURCE_RESULT.md measured independently. THE RECORD IS BACK TO
TWO DEFECTS FOR THE WORLD-NAMING SLOT, NOT THREE: the trained readout mapping concentrates, and the
answer assembly has no composition. No information-loss defect exists.

WHY THIS IS WORTH RECORDING AS A METHOD NOTE. The check was run BEFORE the claim propagated, on my
own headline, by asking what the probe would do if the claim were false. A penalty sweep and a
whitening step cost nothing and take one round. The failure mode to name: A FIXED HYPERPARAMETER
APPLIED ACROSS FEATURE SETS TURNS A CONDITIONING DIFFERENCE INTO AN APPARENT CAPABILITY DIFFERENCE.
The record's other probes use lambda = 1.0 too, and any of them comparing feature sets of different
scaling inherits the same risk.

NOT ESTABLISHED: preserved information does not mean the trained machine works - it reads 1.24%, and
preservation and usability are different claims; lambda -> 0 is not a free lunch and the sweep
identifies conditioning rather than recommending a value; the whitened ^6_trained(x) row is 56.38%,
below the untrained block's 65.96%, so the two checks agree on the untrained block and are less clear
on the trained one; one quantity, one representative slot per packet, rebuilt inputs; one arm, one
seed. No GPU work launched.

## 2026-09-17 UTC - The ^6 block's architecture costs 26 points of decodability, trained or not

$0.00. torch used only to deserialise checkpoints; ridge classification solved in the primal. Script
`records/encoder_capacity_001/measure_information_through_stages.py` (result
`information_through_stages.txt`), record `STAGE_INFORMATION_RESULT.md`.

5-fold cross-validated ridge classification of the focus-world alias through each stage. Chance is
1/64 = 1.5625%.
  x - the encoded slot itself                          63.65%   40.74x
  tanh(x) - the analytic first stage                   63.83%   40.85x
  tanh(left_untrained @ x)                             51.95%   33.25x
  tanh(left_trained @ x)                               48.40%   30.98x
  ^6_untrained(x) - the full block                     37.23%   23.83x
  ^6_trained(x) - the full block                       37.41%   23.94x

Scalar saturation sweep, involving NO trained parameter:
  s      0.25    1.00    8.00   16.00   32.00   64.00   128.00
  acc   52.84%  63.83%  65.78%  65.07%  65.43%  60.11%   56.74%

SATURATION IS NOT THE MECHANISM. The sweep is flat to slightly RISING from s=1 to s=32, peaking at
65.78% at s=8 where the mean absolute pre-activation is 0.82 - well into tanh's curved region. It
only degrades past s=64. So scalar saturation of the encoded slot does not discard the class
information, and the hypothesis that the trained block's 19.73x growth in effective operator norm
destroyed it by saturating tanh is REFUTED.

TRAINING CHANGES THE BLOCK'S INFORMATION CONTENT ALMOST NOT AT ALL: ^6_untrained(x) 37.23% versus
^6_trained(x) 37.41%, a difference of 0.18 percentage points. On how much linearly decodable
information the block preserves about the focus world, 160 AdamW updates changed essentially
nothing. Whatever training did - and CONCENTRATION_SOURCE_RESULT.md shows it concentrated the
readout from 57 distinct aliases to 27 on task-free inputs - it did NOT do it by destroying
information in the block. The trained block's 1.24% readout performance against the untrained's
~6.9% overall is a change in WHAT THE FROZEN READOUT EMITS, not in what the block carries.

THE BLOCK'S OWN ARCHITECTURE IS THE LOSS, AND IT IS LARGE. x 63.65% -> tanh(x) 63.83% (the
elementwise tanh costs nothing) -> tanh(left @ x) 48.40-51.95% (one 64x64 matrix and a tanh costs
12-15 points) -> full ^6 37.2-37.4% (the merge costs 12-15 more). THE REGISTERED ^6 ARCHITECTURE
REDUCES THE FOCUS WORLD'S LINEAR DECODABILITY FROM 63.83% TO 37.3% - A LOSS OF 26.5 POINTS - AND IT
DOES SO WHETHER ITS WEIGHTS ARE TRAINED OR RANDOM. That gives a CEILING for the trained run which
nothing in that run could have exceeded: a free linear readout on ^6_trained(x) reaches 37.41%, so
no readout of any kind, however well trained, could have read the focus world through this block
above roughly 37%. The learner reached 1.24%.

THIS IS WHY THE ANALYTIC INITIALISATION WORKS. It sets left = I64, right = 0, merge = [T | 0], so
^6(x) = T tanh(x): the right branch contributes tanh(0) = 0 and its half of merge is zero, so the
two-branch merge COLLAPSES TO A SINGLE LINEAR MAP with no lossful composition. And tanh(x) measures
63.83%, identical to the input. So the analytic initialisation does not merely fit a good T - IT
BYPASSES THE BLOCK'S INFORMATION LOSS ENTIRELY, which is a second independent reason it reaches
62.94% direct where the trained block's ceiling is 37%.

THE TRAINED MACHINE FAILS TWICE OVER: the block costs it 26 points before the readout is consulted,
and the frozen readout then extracts almost none of the 37 that remain, because training
concentrated its emissions.

WHAT IT MEANS FOR THE ARCHITECTURE. A 64 -> 64 -> 128 -> 64 block with two tanh branches and a
merge, as registered, DISCARDS 42% OF THE FOCUS WORLD'S LINEARLY AVAILABLE INFORMATION. That is a
measured architectural cost, present at initialisation and unchanged by training - not a training
defect, not a loss defect, not an encoder defect. The analytic initialisation is in effect the
degenerate case of that architecture in which the second branch is switched off, and it is worth 26
points. That is a strong hint that the two-branch structure is not earning its cost on this task.

NO CHANGE IS PROPOSED. This record measures a cost; it does not propose removing the branch, and any
such change is a contract question - the ^6 contract fixes neural_transform_id = cas.op.decompose.v1.

NOT ESTABLISHED: nothing was scored by the machine's own checker - these are free-readout
decodability figures, not the whole-answer metric; the encoded slot is rebuilt at +0.781 correlation
with the recorded logits, so the comparisons BETWEEN rows are valid and no absolute number is the
machine's; 37.41% is a ceiling for a LINEAR readout, not for every readout, and nothing establishes
that 37% is the maximum attainable; the loss is measured on one quantity, the focus-world alias at
one representative slot per packet; one arm, one seed; no claim that the two-branch structure is
wrong, since it may carry information this measurement does not probe and it is what
cas.op.decompose.v1 defines. No GPU work launched.

## 2026-09-17 UTC - The concentration is the training's, the readout is nearly uniform, and the analytic fit does not share it

$0.00. torch used only to deserialise checkpoints and read the frozen public course. Script
`records/encoder_capacity_001/measure_concentration_source.py` (result `concentration_source.txt`),
record `CONCENTRATION_SOURCE_RESULT.md`.

210 pseudo-random unit vectors - inputs carrying no task information - through four
parameterisations on the same recurrence, the same frozen readout, the same inputs. THE ENCODER IS
NEVER TOUCHED, so the +0.781 reconstruction gap limits nothing here.

  parameterisation                    depth  distinct  commonest  share  entropy
  identity - NO BLOCK AT ALL, f(x)=x      1        59         W5    3.3%    5.752
  identity - NO BLOCK AT ALL              8        59         W5    3.3%    5.752
  untrained, cursor 0                     1        58        W44    4.8%    5.586
  untrained, cursor 0                     8        57        W36    4.8%    5.535
  trained, cursor 160                     1        44        W47   10.0%    4.853
  trained, cursor 160                     8        27        W40   25.2%    3.752
  analytic - arm A                        1        24        W15   10.0%    4.394
  analytic - arm A                        8        24        W22    8.6%    4.404
  uniform, no machine at all               -        64          -    1.6%    6.000
  RECORDED, real slots, cursor 160         8         9        W36   44.3%    2.516

THE FROZEN READOUT'S GEOMETRY IS NEARLY UNIFORM. The identity row involves NO PARAMETER - f(x) = x
and the argmax is taken directly - and gives 59 distinct aliases, 3.3% commonest, 5.752 bits,
against the uniform 64 / 1.6% / 6.000. So the frozen Rademacher readout is NOT inherently biased
toward a few symbols; whatever concentration the machine shows is produced by the parameters, not by
the codebook's geometry. That rules out one whole family of explanations and is why the identity row
was worth running.

TRAINING IS THE CAUSE AND THE RECURRENCE AMPLIFIES IT. The untrained block leaves the distribution
nearly as spread as the identity (58 -> 57 distinct, 4.8%). The trained block more than doubles the
commonest share at depth 1 (4.8% -> 10.0%) and the recurrence more than doubles it again
(10.0% -> 25.2%). All of it on inputs the block has never seen.

THE ANALYTIC FIT DOES NOT SHARE IT. Same inputs, depth 8: trained gives 27 distinct with the
commonest at 25.2% and 3.752 bits; ANALYTIC gives 24 distinct with the commonest at 8.6% and 4.404
bits. The counts are comparable and the SHARES ARE NOT - 25.2% against 8.6%, 0.65 bits apart. The
trained block returns to one alias a quarter of the time whatever it is given; the analytic
parameters do so under a tenth of the time. And the analytic fit is STABLE THROUGH THE RECURRENCE
where the trained block is not: trained 4.853 -> 3.752 bits (-1.101), analytic 4.394 -> 4.404
(+0.010). The recurrence costs the analytic fit nothing at all on task-free inputs.

AND THE TRAINED BLOCK COLLAPSES FURTHER ON REAL INPUTS WHILE THE ANALYTIC FIT DOES NOT:
  trained, depth 8    task-free 27 distinct / 25.2%   real 9 distinct / 44.3% (recorded)
  analytic, depth 8   task-free 24 distinct /  8.6%   real 24 distinct /  6.9% (out-of-fold)
The analytic fit behaves the SAME on both. The trained block behaves very differently - spread-ish on
invented inputs, severely concentrated on real ones. So the trained block's concentration is driven
specifically by the TASK DISTRIBUTION: it loses variety precisely where the task lives.

WHAT THIS DOES FOR ARM A. G6's prediction is now supported by an ENCODER-FREE measurement, not only
by the rebuilt encoder, and both directions agree: the analytic parameters do not concentrate the
way the trained block does. It also adds a prediction the preparation could not make - the
recurrence costs the analytic fit +0.010 bits and the trained block -1.101 bits, so if arm A runs
and the recurrence turns out costly for it, that contradicts this measurement and not merely the
approximate rebuild.

NOT ESTABLISHED: nothing was scored - entropy and share are distributional facts and say nothing
about correctness, and THE TARGETS ARE SEALED. A SPREAD READOUT IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A RIGHT ONE:
the analytic fit spreads while reaching 62.06% and the trained block concentrates while reaching
1.24%, but nothing here shows the spread CAUSES the accuracy. The task-free inputs are invented. The
analytic T here is a single fold's fit, not the arm's per-fold artifact. The four rows are not
independent - each adds to the one above. One arm, one seed. Depth 1 and 8 only. And the
concentration is still not shown to be HARMFUL; nothing here establishes that reducing it improves
anything. No GPU work launched.

## 2026-09-17 UTC - The alias concentration has three separable causes, and training is the largest

$0.00. torch used only to deserialise checkpoints; all arithmetic on plain lists. Script
`records/encoder_capacity_001/measure_input_independence.py` (result `input_independence.txt`),
record `INPUT_INDEPENDENCE_RESULT.md`; `COLLAPSE_CLAIM_WITHDRAWN.md` annotated PARTLY RESOLVED.

THIS TEST NEVER TOUCHES THE ENCODER, so it escapes the +0.781 reconstruction gap entirely. The
block, the recurrence and the frozen readout are all exact for the saved weights and the frozen
rows, so feeding INVENTED inputs through them is exact arithmetic on the real trained parameters.
210 inputs per regime, both checkpoints, depth 1 and depth 8:

  checkpoint  depth                     regime   distinct  commonest  share  entropy
   cursor 0      1   pseudo-random unit vectors        58       W44    4.8%    5.586
   cursor 0      1   one constant vector                1       W58  100.0%    0.000
   cursor 0      8   pseudo-random unit vectors        57       W36    4.8%    5.535
   cursor 0      8   one constant vector                1       W58  100.0%    0.000
 cursor 160      1   pseudo-random unit vectors        44       W47   10.0%    4.853
 cursor 160      1   one constant vector                1       W26  100.0%    0.000
 cursor 160      8   pseudo-random unit vectors        27       W40   25.2%    3.752
 cursor 160      8   one constant vector                1       W40  100.0%    0.000

For reference: uniform over 64 aliases is 64 distinct, 1.6%, 6.000 bits. The recorded distribution
over the real world-typed reference slots at cursor 160 is 9 distinct, W36 at 44.3%, 2.516 bits. The
constant-input row is the control: one input, one output, 1 distinct, 100%, and what it says when
told nothing differs between checkpoints (W58 untrained, W40 trained).

THE ANSWER TO THE OPEN QUESTION. Random unit vectors carry no task information, so they isolate what
the WEIGHTS do alone, and the chain is monotone:
  uniform, no machine at all                    64 distinct   1.6%   6.000
  untrained block, one pass                     58 distinct   4.8%   5.586
  + the recurrence                              57 distinct   4.8%   5.535
  + TRAINING the block                          44 distinct  10.0%   4.853
  + the recurrence again                        27 distinct  25.2%   3.752
  + the real encoded slots (recorded)            9 distinct  44.3%   2.516
TRAINING ALONE moves the readout from 58 distinct aliases to 44 and from 4.8% to 10.0% on the
commonest, on inputs it has never seen. The recurrence takes it to 27 and 25.2%; the real inputs
take it the rest of the way. So the concentration is NOT caused by the inputs and NOT by the
encoder. The largest single contributor is the training, visible with inputs carrying no information
whatsoever.

THE APPARENT CONTRADICTION FROM ROUNDS 10 AND 11 IS RESOLVED. Round 10 claimed the trained block
collapsed onto an input-independent fixed point. Round 11 refuted that: the weights GREW (effective
operator norm 0.361559 -> 7.135410, x19.73) and the block became MORE input-sensitive (mean pairwise
cosine 0.563576 -> 0.491361, rms-from-mean 0.028791 -> 0.280260), and the claim was withdrawn. BOTH
MEASUREMENTS ARE CORRECT AND THIS TABLE SHOWS HOW THEY FIT: the trained block's output vector varies
MORE across inputs while the READOUT'S ARGMAX varies LESS - 58 distinct aliases to 44 at depth 1
and 57 to 27 at depth 8, on identical random inputs. Training makes the output larger and more
variable AND pushes it into a region where the frozen readout's argmax is decided by fewer of its
rows. The vector spreads; what the readout says narrows. Round 10 was wrong about the mechanism and
right about the effect.

WHAT IT MEANS FOR THE ARCHITECTURE. The concentration is a property of the trained weights,
measured on task-free inputs, which places it in the plastic block and its interaction with the
frozen readout. It is NOT an encoder defect, so no encoder change addresses it; NOT a data-coverage
defect, so more course variety does not address it; and it IS visible at depth 1, so it is the
block's own doing with the recurrence as an amplifier rather than the origin. It does NOT say the
concentration is harmful - a concentrated readout is not automatically a wrong one, and nothing here
measures correctness, because the targets are sealed. What it says is that the machine's answer at a
world-typed slot is substantially determined BEFORE ITS INPUT IS CONSULTED, and an architecture
review should account for that.

NOT ESTABLISHED: nothing was scored - concentration is a distributional fact, not an error rate;
regimes 2 and 3 are invented inputs that isolate the weights and are not a claim about the course;
the causes are separated but not attributed quantitatively and the stages are not independent; one
arm, one seed (MIXED_SAME_ITEMS, 20260919); depth 1 and 8 only; the commonest alias under random
inputs is W40, not the recorded W36, so the weights' own preference and the recorded outcome are
related but not identical, and the difference is the input's contribution. No GPU work launched.
